| `credentials.expired` keeps its name, with a `reason`, `refused` or `missing` | A new event | The harness does the same in both cases: put the secret again, or approve the call. |

### Taken for the object-storage rewrite (November 2026)

Reviewed by the owner with diagrams on 22 November 2026: every loop that asked a store or the API
whether something changed, the writes made when nothing happened, the event-driven target, and the
code contracts. The owner asked that every part of Rewind scale, with compute apart from storage
on object storage, that only live runs can lock anything, that finished runs never go through
Postgres, and that nothing polls but the scheduler.

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A store is written only when a state changes**: a fact, an owner that moves, a setting someone changes; never a lease renewal, a heartbeat, a queue position or a poll (W1) | Leases, claims and a feed in Postgres | The owner's rule. An idle worker sent about 112 queries a second; one tool call wrote 25 rows, of which 8 were facts. |
| **A tool call costs its run two appends**: the call before it is sent, its outcome before the script sees it (W2) | Writing the claim, its renewals and its release | The rest is the owner's memory, rebuilt from the facts after a takeover. |
| **Runs live in partitions on object storage**: one SlateDB database per partition on R2, one writer per partition, batches shared by every run of the partition. Postgres holds the control plane only, Turso when embedded | Postgres for runs; Postgres sharded | Compute scales apart from storage; a batch costs one write to R2 whatever the number of runs in it; Postgres stays at the pace of people. |
| **R2** for the bucket | Tigris on Railway | The owner's choice. |
| **No broker** (E1). Facts are the partition's records; messages go between nodes over the node link; a lost message is rebuilt from the facts at takeover, never caught by asking again | NATS JetStream or Redpanda between every actor | One system less to run, and the records are already the source of truth. |
| **The SDK gets a task stream** (E2); the open stream is the liveness of the tasks handed on it; per-task heartbeats are gone. SDK 0.5.0 drops the long-poll (C8) | The long-poll and heartbeats | Nothing is written or asked while a task is in flight. Dax, the one harness running, moves in the same release. |
| **A start above an organization's ceiling is refused with 429** (E3) | Holding the start in the organization's queue | Holding it would add a queued state to runs. The organization actor admits starts in order, so a few milliseconds of latency are accepted. |
| **An attempt is sealed to the archive when it ends** (E4) and read from there, cached forever; a retry or a fork reads it back into its partition | 30 days in Postgres, then archived | Ended runs never go through Postgres, and a sealed journal never changes. |
| **The dashboard runs on the change stream alone** (E5): no view reads on a timer; this replaces the temporary change loop and the 60 s refresh of the [live dashboard](#taken-for-the-live-dashboard-november-2026) | Keeping the loop | The owner's rule. |
| **Polling cannot come back** (E6): `rewind-architecture-tests` refuses `interval` and `sleep` outside the timer wheel, the backoff helpers and the clocks that read no store; the dashboard's lint refuses `refetchInterval` | Discipline | Tooling enforces the boundaries. |
| **A dead node is noticed through Postgres** (E7): each node holds a session lock on its own id, and the next node of a ring waits on it | A lease row renewed every 10 s | Nothing is written while nothing happens; SlateDB's writer epoch still fences a node that was only cut off. |
| **No analytics engine** (E8, C5): lists and searches come from the run index in each partition, with the same query language and a scan budget; statistics from hourly counters, by namespace and window only | ClickHouse | The owner: "we can do without for now". Statistics on an arbitrary query wait for an analytics engine reading the archive. |
| **Retries are not landed first** (C1) | Merging the retries' branch before the rewrite | The owner's choice. The records carry an attempt, so retries fit without a migration. |
| **The runs already in Postgres are migrated once** (C2): `rewind server migrate-runs` moves the ended runs to the archive and the live ones into their partitions | Draining and starting empty | Dax's runs on staging survive the deploy. |
| **64 partitions** (C3), 1 embedded; a namespace's partition is chosen when it is created and never changes; a run id carries its partition in 16 bits of its UUID v7 | Partitions by run; moving namespaces | A namespace's keys, lists and counters stay in one partition; any process routes a run from its id. |
| **Payloads belong to their run** (C4): up to 64 KiB in the partition and inlined in the sealed journal, above in blobs under the run; shared only within a run; a fork copies what it references | Payloads deduplicated per tenant, with their references and cleanup | No cleanup job is left; erasing a run deletes its prefix. The cost is duplicate bytes when runs carry the same large value. |
| **One kind of process** (C6): every process is the HTTP edge and a node; Railway scales it by replicas; the node link is HTTP/2 with JSON on the private network | An api role and a worker role | One thing to deploy. |
| **The embedded server is one node** (C7): one partition, SlateDB on local files, Turso for the control plane, the node link in process | A separate embedded store | The CLI runs the code production runs. |
| **The control plane notifies its changes** (C9): every write sends its change in the same transaction (`NOTIFY`), and every process listens | Reading the control plane on every request | Caches stay right without a timer; the dashboard's stream carries scripts, workflows, secrets, namespaces and connections. |
| **SlateDB 0.18** (C10), its compactor in the writer's process, the WAL flushed by the committer after each batch | Another embedded store on object storage | Writer fencing, atomic write batches and conditional puts on R2. Its manifest reads on a timer are measured in the R2 benchmark (W4). |

## Architecture (approved for 0.1)

| Decision | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Strict layering: domain, application, adapters, composition roots | The core must not know how events are stored. Boundaries are enforced in code. |
| **Ports live in `rewind-engine`**, not in `rewind-core` | Core stays a model; the engine says what it needs from outside. |
| **No serde at all in `rewind-core`**; `rewind-format` owns the stored format (v1) | A refactor of domain types can never break the journal of a paused run. Costs a conversion layer. |
| Core knows payload identity (value or digest), not location | Inline vs S3, keys, sizes and previews are storage concerns. |
| Ports shaped by invariants (`commit_activation`) | The commit invariant is guaranteed by every store, not re-implemented by callers. |
| The runtime only executes; replay lives in the engine | The V8 adapter stays small and swappable; replay logic is tested without V8. |
| Non-determinism only through ports | The same engine is production, mock mode and the test harness. |
| Dependency rules checked by a test | Boundaries hold without relying on discipline. |
| Review the architecture principles of each contract and design, with diagrams, before the code that depends on it | Process rule. |
| **The journal is the record; OTel, SSE and the dashboard are projections** of committed entries | One source of truth. Replay can never duplicate telemetry, and the dashboard isn't limited by OTel sampling or retention. |
| Service telemetry through the `tracing` facade; OpenTelemetry only in `rewind-otel`, installed by composition roots; nothing in `rewind-core` | The engine stays free of vendor SDKs; exporters are a deployment choice. |
| A read side separate from `JournalStore`: the `ReadModel` port, filled by a projector | `JournalStore` stays shaped by the write path's invariants; queries across runs get their own tables. |
| The dashboard is a client of the public HTTP API | The SDK, the agent tools and the dashboard see the same thing; no back door into the store. |
| Run metadata (labels, trace context) stored with the run, outside the journal's events | The script never sees it, so it can't affect determinism, and it never ends up in cassettes. |

Approved in the design review of November 2026. They shape the stored format, the
runtime's observable behavior or the port contracts, so they are frozen with format v1:

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Determinism epoch** in `run_started`. Glue, generators, library and codegen are kept per epoch in the binary; V8 and ICU get a worker pool per epoch | Pinning the crate version and refusing the runs of another version | The script is immutable, the runtime is not: a change in V8, the glue or codegen can shift a `seq` or a digest of a suspended run. |
| **A replay mismatch quarantines the run** | `run_aborted { non_determinism }` at once | By construction it is a fault of the platform, never of the script. The run waits for a fix. |
| **A script's terminal command waits for its tool calls** | Dropping pending calls when `main` settles | A forgotten `await` becomes harmless, and a run that ends by itself has an outcome for every call, which a fork needs. Costs: a raced slow tool is still awaited. |
| **An unhandled rejection fails the run** | Ignoring it | Node's behavior. Ignoring it hid failed calls. |
| **Idle with nothing pending fails the run with `DeadlockError`** | Leaving the run active | Nothing can wake the script. It is the script's own outcome, reproduced by replay, so `fail_run` and not `run_aborted`. |
| **256-bit seed; ChaCha20 for `crypto`**, sfc32 kept for `Math.random` | sfc32 with a 64-bit seed for both | Scripts make tokens and passwords with `crypto`, and they were predictable. |
| **Canonical JSON is RFC 8785** | "Keys sorted, no whitespace" | Digests are compared for as long as runs live; that wording left numbers and key order to the serializer. |
| **Idempotency keys for starting a run and for signals** | Keys on tool calls only | A retried request must not start a second run, nor leave a second signal in the mailbox. |

## Technical (open to challenge)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Durability by event-sourced replay | V8 heap snapshots | V8 can't snapshot a running isolate with pending promises, and snapshots break on every V8 upgrade. |
| Inbox + single writer per run (lease + fence) | Writing completions straight into the history | Makes history order deterministic and replay exact. |
| Commit invariant; `run_aborted` distinct from `fail_run` | Committing triggers and commands separately | Replay never sees a half-processed trigger; host failures are not replayed as script failures. |
| Ops are synchronous; promises resolved by the JS glue | Async ops on deno_core's event loop | No hidden scheduling; delivery order is fully controlled. |
| Logical time = `recorded_at` of the last delivered trigger, monotonic per run | Real time, or time recorded per call | Time moves only when the outside world speaks, identically on replay. |
| Seeded generators for `Math.random` and `crypto` | Journaling each random value | No journal entries for randomness; the seed is in `run_started`. |
| `TZ=UTC` and `en-US` locale enforced; sandbox refuses to start otherwise | Host-dependent `Intl` | Identical output on every machine. |
| Signals: one FIFO mailbox per name; timeouts are durable timers cancelled on arrival | Drop signals nobody waits for | Nothing is lost if the signal arrives before the script waits. |
| Content addressing everywhere (SHA-256; canonical JSON for payloads) | Random ids for scripts and payloads | Deduplication, cacheability, and digest comparison during replay. |
| Inline threshold 1 KiB | Everything in S3 | Keeps tiny payloads one read away. Configurable. The tiers above it are an owner decision ([payloads](06-storage-and-scale.md#payloads)). |
| Postgres + an embedded store behind one port with a shared conformance suite | One backend | Scale in production, zero setup locally. |
| V8 instance on a dedicated thread behind `ScriptExecutor` | Running V8 on the async runtime | V8 objects are `!Send`; the engine stays a normal async crate. |
| Ports as traits returning `impl Future + Send`; engine generic over adapters | `dyn` traits with `async_trait` | Static dispatch, no boxing; composition roots pick types. |
| Tool policies `retry` / `at_most_once`, idempotency key `{run_id}/{seq}` | Always retry | A crash mid-call must not silently duplicate a non-idempotent effect. |
| Single-file scripts, imports only from `rewind` and `rewind:tools` | npm imports | Determinism and supply chain. Curated libraries later. |
| Tool names kept as defined by the server | camelCase renaming | They must match the descriptions the model reads. |
| Callsites mapped to TypeScript lines through source maps | JS positions | Traces and errors point at the code the model wrote. |
| Projector fed by feed items written in `commit_activation`, one per consumer, claimed and acknowledged | An in-process hook after commit; a high-water-mark cursor on a sequence | Nothing lost on a crash; Postgres sequences are not visible in commit order; a slow OTel backend doesn't delay the dashboard. |
| Trace id = the harness's trace id if given, else the run id; span ids derived from `(run, seq)`, `(run, fence)`, `(run, idx)` | Random span ids | A span exported twice is the same span; live service spans can name journal spans as parents. |
| Run span exported when the run ends, with an id known from the start | A span open for the whole run; no run span at all | Durable runs outlive spans. Short runs still appear as a complete tree. |
| A run with a `traceparent` joins the harness's trace | A new trace per run, with a span link | The run shows up under the LLM call that wrote it. Costs very long traces for long runs. |
| Spans carry payload digests, sizes and previews, never values | Full payloads as attributes | Model-written arguments may hold sensitive data; OTel backends have weaker access control and attribute size limits. |
| Sampling per run, from the trace id; the read model is never sampled | Sampling per activation | Every worker agrees without coordination; the dashboard stays complete. |
| Logs exported as OTel log records | Span events | They need no open span and correlate with the activation span. |
| W3C trace context forwarded to MCP servers in `_meta` | No propagation | MCP servers' own spans join the run's trace. |
| The `rewind` binary is its own crate (entry point), depending on `rewind-cli` and `rewind-server` | The binary target in `rewind-cli`, linking the server | Nothing else depends on a composition root, and the CLI reaches the server only through the API. |
| The HTTP DTOs, the OpenAPI document and a thin Rust HTTP client live in `rewind-api` (adapter support, depends only on `rewind-core`), shared by `rewind-server` and `rewind-cli` | DTOs in `rewind-server` | The CLI is an API client and cannot depend on a composition root. |

### Taken while implementing the layering (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ECMAScript number formatting for RFC 8785 through `ryu-js`, added to the dependencies `rewind-core` may have | Our own formatter on top of Rust's shortest float printing | Rust's `{:e}` rounds exact ties up; ECMAScript and RFC 8785 round them to even (`1424953923781206.25` → `…206.2`). A runtime test compares the core's canonical numbers with V8's on thousands of random doubles. |
| `serde_json` with `float_roundtrip` in the whole workspace | Its default float parser | The default parser can be one ULP off (`333333333.33333329`): a value going through JS and Rust would change, and its digest with it. |
| The stored format writes every JSON value in its canonical form | The JSON library's key order | The stored bytes no longer depend on a feature some dependency enables (`preserve_order`), and they are exactly the bytes a payload's digest is computed on. |
| Dynamic `import()` is refused when the script compiles | Refusing it in the module loader | There is no event loop in the sandbox: the import would never settle, and the script would end with a misleading `DeadlockError`. |
| The checks at start run on the syntax tree (swc): imported modules and names, default export | Loading the module in a throwaway isolate | Cheap and deterministic, with no script code running before the run exists. What only shows when the script runs (a top-level exception) still aborts the run with `invalid_script` at its first activation. |
| The CPU budget applies to each call into the script | A budget per activation | An activation can replay the full history: a long run must not exhaust its budget replaying it. |
| Each generator gets its own key, `SHA-256(domain ‖ seed)`; `crypto` is the ChaCha20 keystream of RFC 8439 with a zero nonce | Keying both generators with the seed | What `Math.random` reveals tells nothing about `crypto`, and the keystream is a standard one. |
| Unhandled rejections are read from deno_core's queue of rejections without a handler, drained after each microtask checkpoint | Our own `PromiseRejectCallback` | deno_core already tracks rejections, and removes those handled later in the same checkpoint (Node's semantics). |
| Errors created by the glue have no glue frames in their stack, and `fail_run` drops glue frames | Raw V8 stacks | The glue's line numbers would otherwise leak into journals and change them on every edit of the glue. |
| Each instance runs on its own thread with a single-threaded tokio runtime (for module loading), next to a watchdog thread | One thread per isolate pool | Simplest correct model. Kept at the target scale with the bounded [sticky cache](03-execution.md#sticky-execution) (owner decision above). |
| The epoch corpus lives in a tooling crate, `rewind-e2e`: blue cases must succeed, red cases must fail exactly as specified; journals are compared byte for byte and replayed from their files | Corpus inside `rewind-cli` | The corpus does not need the CLI, and the CLI's end-to-end tests reuse the same cases. |
| Values reach the script with their keys in canonical order, live and on replay | Keeping the order they arrived in | Stored values are canonical: a run would see one order live and another on replay, diverge, and be quarantined while healthy. Found by review. |
| An activation that fails for any reason other than the script (a missing blob, a store error, a replay interrupted by a limit or an internal error) keeps its lease and is retried when it expires | Releasing the lease at once; quarantining | The run picked first is the one with the oldest inbox item: released at once, it would be picked again and again and block every other run. A transient failure must not stop a healthy run for good. Found by review. After 5 failures in a row the run is quarantined (owner decision above). |
| The real `Date` stays private to the glue; only `Date.parse` and `Date.UTC` are copied | Making the logical `Date` inherit from it | Through `Object.getPrototypeOf(Date)` a script read the real clock. Found by review. |
| After a cancellation, a script that nothing can wake fails with `CancelledError`, not `DeadlockError` | `DeadlockError` | Cancelling a wait built on `setTimeout` drops the timer: the run must still end `cancelled`. Found by review. |
| Lone surrogates become U+FFFD when a value leaves the script | Refusing the value | A string cut in the middle of an emoji is common in generated code; no host string can hold half of a pair. Found by review. |

### Taken while freezing the HTTP API and the sandbox lib (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The sandbox lib lives in `crates/rewind-runtime/lib/`: `rewind.d.ts`, `globals.d.ts`, `fetch.d.ts`, `tsconfig.json` and `globals.json` (the declared names, the `rewind` exports, the hints for absent globals). `rewind-runtime` embeds it (`sandbox_lib`); `rewind-check` embeds the same files by path with `include_str!`. It declares the latest epoch | A copy in `rewind-check`; a crate of its own | The runtime is what the lib describes, and two adapters cannot depend on each other. Scripts are only type-checked when a run starts, and a run starts under the latest epoch. |
| The checker's ECMAScript lib is ES2024 as tsgo's granular libs, without the `sharedmemory` libs (es2017, es2020, es2024) and `es2021.weakref`, plus `esnext.temporal` | `"lib": ["es2024"]` | The sandbox removes `SharedArrayBuffer`, `Atomics`, `WeakRef` and `FinalizationRegistry`, and has `Temporal`. Checked with TypeScript 7.0.2. |
| `ToolError` has a private constructor in `rewind.d.ts`; timer callbacks take `any` arguments, as on the web | The declaration of 01 word for word; typed timer arguments | Only the glue builds a `ToolError`: `new ToolError("x")` would throw. `setTimeout(resolve, 100)` inside `new Promise` must check. |
| `fetch` is declared only with the `http` tool, as a subset of the web's: a string or `URL`; `method`, `headers`, `body`; a `Response` with its status, headers and body as text, JSON, `ArrayBuffer` or bytes. `Request`, `Response` and `Headers` are types, not globals | The full WHATWG `fetch` | It is a journaled tool call: no streams, no `AbortSignal`, redirects followed by the dispatcher. |
| API JSON: camelCase fields, snake_case enumerated values, RFC 3339 times (`jiff`), durations as scripts write them or in milliseconds. Request bodies refuse unknown fields | snake_case as in the journal; milliseconds everywhere | MCP and TypeScript are camelCase, so the SDK's generated types need no mapping. A field a model misspells is refused, not ignored. |
| Problem types are `urn:rewind:problem:<slug>`, 16 kinds (`rewind_api::ProblemType`); `invalid-script` carries `diagnostics`; an error body that is not problem+json reads as `about:blank` | URLs on a domain | No domain to own; the URN is stable and never dereferenced. |
| Outbound webhooks follow Standard Webhooks: `webhook-id`, `webhook-timestamp`, `webhook-signature: v1,<base64 HMAC-SHA256>`, secrets `whsec_…`, 5 minutes of tolerance; written with `hmac` and `sha2` | Our own header; the `standardwebhooks` crate | Every language has a verifier for that specification. The crate is unmaintained since 2024. |
| Lifecycle events add `run.cancelled`, `run.aborted` and `run.quarantined` to those of 07 | Only `run.completed`, `run.failed`, `run.suspended` | The harness must learn of every end of a run, and of a quarantine (owner decision: reported through webhooks). |
| `POST /hooks/{token}`: a JSON body is the payload, another body a string, an empty one `null`; the delivery id is the first of `Idempotency-Key`, `webhook-id`, `X-GitHub-Delivery` | One header only | Senders name their delivery ids differently. |
| Endpoints added to 07's table: `POST /v1/tasks/{token}/defer`, `GET /v1/scripts/{digest}`, `GET` and `DELETE /v1/subscriptions`, `DELETE /v1/api-keys/{id}`, `GET /v1/server`, `POST /v1/server/stop`. `fork` takes `Idempotency-Key` | — | The `deferred` state, the dashboard's source view, revocable keys and subscriptions, and the CLI's version check each need a call. A fork creates a run. |
| API keys have one of four roles: `admin` (keys of every tenant), `harness`, `worker` (host-tool tasks only), `reader` (`GET` only) | A single role | The SDK worker and the dashboard should not hold a harness's rights. Superseded by [accounts and access](#taken-for-accounts-organizations-and-access-november-2026). |
| A payload is shown as `{digest, bytes, preview, value?}`; the value is left out above 256 KiB, except in `GET /v1/runs/{id}/entries/{idx}` | Values always inline | Run states, streams and webhooks stay small; 256 KiB is the bound of `run_script`. |
| Lists in query strings repeat their key (`label=agent:triage&label=…`), read with `serde_html_form` (axum-extra's `Query`) | Comma-separated values | Label values may hold commas. |
| The OpenAPI operations are declared in `rewind-api` by bodiless functions; their paths are `rewind_api::routes`, which the server's router uses; tests check both name the same operations, and that every route fits one axum router | Annotating the server's handlers | The document is a contract written before the server, and the CLI depends on it, never on the server. |
| The client is `reqwest` (rustls, unix sockets natively), with `sse-stream` for the event stream | hyper with `hyperlocal`; `eventsource-stream` | One client for both transports; `sse-stream` is maintained, and `rmcp` uses it. |
| A deployed server refuses stdio MCP servers unless its configuration allows them; the embedded server accepts them | Accepting any command | A stdio server is a process the tenant chooses, started by Rewind. |

### Taken while freezing the contracts in code (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A toolset's digest is the SHA-256 of its pinned toolset document v1 in canonical form, `"v": 1` included; `rewind-core` builds the same JSON, and `rewind-format` writes exactly those bytes | A digest over a core-only shape | A store can check a stored toolset against its key, as it does a blob. The e2e journals were recorded again: only `run_started.toolset` changed. |
| A tool is named `{namespace}.{name}`: its MCP server, or its host-tool group (optional); built-ins are named `http` and `webhook`; namespaces are identifiers | Free-form names with a separate namespace field | `rewind:tools` exports one namespace per server or group, and a name says where a call goes. |
| A tool's `concurrency` caps the calls of that tool in flight in one run (16 by default); the server's caps the calls in flight to it per worker (8). In flight: claimed and not expired, or deferred | A single cap per run | The harness can serialize one tool (`ask_user`: 1) without slowing the others. The store enforces both at claim, from values copied into the task. |
| A claim lasts the policy's heartbeat timeout (60 s by default, every route); host tools have no execution timeout by default, the others 5 min | A lease duration chosen by the claimer | One rule for the dispatcher and the harness's workers; host tools may last days. |
| One token per claim; a claim taken again refuses the old token; a deferred task keeps its token until answered | One token per task | A worker that lost its claim learns it at its next heartbeat, and only the live attempt reports (the tool sees the same idempotency key either way). |
| `acquire_runnable` takes the random `draw` (tenants with work ordered by id, index `draw` modulo their count); the sticky grace counts from the oldest inbox item | The store drawing at random | Stores stay deterministic and the fairness of the draw is testable. A run left by a dead worker is taken over at once. |
| Quarantine records its kind (`non_determinism`, `failures`) and the last build that retried it; `acquire_quarantined` gives each run to one worker of a new build, once | The reason alone | Giving up must write `non_determinism` or `internal`, and several workers of one build must not all retry a run. |
| Retention and payload cleanup apply each tenant's settings inside the store (`runs_to_archive(now)`, `unreferenced_payloads(now)`); an archive is a journal document in the `BlobStore`, under the SHA-256 of its bytes; `BlobStore::put` refuses bytes that do not hash to their key | A threshold computed by the caller per tenant | The tenant without a record (the embedded `local`) gets the defaults with no special case; one content-addressed store holds payloads and archives. |
| A cassette writes args, results and payloads as plain JSON values, one call or signal per line; a signal records `elapsed_ms` since the start; calls without an outcome are left out. The cassette model lives in `rewind-core`, derived from a history | `{"value": …}` wrappers; the cassette in `rewind-format` only | Cassettes are written and edited by people; mock mode in `rewind-engine` needs the model without depending on the format. |
| Observed result types are a `Shape` in `rewind-core` whose merge is commutative, associative and idempotent; the read model folds held results into it | Shapes kept by the projector | The projection is at least once and in any order; codegen reads shapes through `ReadModel::observed_shapes`. |
| Three feed consumers: `read_model`, `otel` and `webhooks` | Lifecycle webhooks sent after commit | A webhook is a projection of the journal like the others: nothing is lost on a crash. `run.quarantined` joins the lifecycle events; `run.failed` covers failed, cancelled and aborted runs. |
| Workers and claims take a `TenantScope` (all, only, except); a claim's `TaskFilter` is `All` (mock mode), `Dispatched` (MCP and built-ins) or `Host` (a tenant's host tools, by toolset and names) | Filters added per backend | Per-tenant worker pools and the host-tool long-poll need them at the port. |
| Tenant ids are 1 to 64 ASCII letters, digits, `-` and `_`; API key roles are strings the store keeps and the server interprets | Free-form tenants; roles in the engine | Tenants appear in blob paths; roles belong to the HTTP API. |
| `Toolset::from_host_tools` builds host tools with the default policy and an input schema that accepts anything, for mock mode and tests; the e2e scenarios use it | Keeping a list of names as a toolset | Every toolset is pinned; in mock mode every call goes to the mocks whatever its route. |

### Taken while building the TypeScript SDK (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sdk/typescript` is a pnpm package of its own (`@rewind.codes/sdk`, its own lockfile), ESM only, Node 22.12 or later, checked and built with TypeScript 7.0.2, tested with vitest. Its types come from `openapi-typescript` 7.13 (run with `typescript` 5.9 through `pnpm dlx`) with `--empty-objects-unknown`; requests go through `openapi-fetch`, the event stream through `eventsource-parser`, webhook verification through `standardwebhooks` | A pnpm workspace at the root; a hand-written fetch client; our own SSE parser and HMAC check | Without the flag, every `{"type": "object"}` of the document (`inputSchema`, cassettes, journals) becomes `Record<string, never>` and nothing can be passed. `openapi-fetch` checks every path, parameter and body against the generated types. `standardwebhooks` is the specification's reference library, maintained again since August 2026, unlike its Rust crate. |
| The SDK depends on no model SDK: the Anthropic and OpenAI tool formats are structural types of its own, checked at compile time against `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and `openai` (dev dependencies only) | Depending on both SDKs | A harness uses one model API; it must not install the other. |
| A `wait` above 50 s is split into several requests (`POST /v1/runs` or `/fork`, then `GET /v1/runs/{id}?wait=` with the time left), and a server that answers early is asked again at most once a second | One request for the whole wait | Proxies cut long requests, and Node's `fetch` gives up on headers after 300 s. |
| The client sends a request again after a network error or a 503 (twice by default) only when that is safe: reads, `PUT`, `DELETE`, toolset registration (content-addressed), check, test, and requests carrying an `Idempotency-Key`. A claim of tasks is never sent again | Retrying everything; retrying nothing | A start or a signal without a key would be duplicated; the lost answer of a claim holds tasks claimed for nobody. |
| The worker sends a heartbeat every third of the task's heartbeat timeout (100 ms at least). A thrown `HostToolError` is the tool's failure (`retryable` opt-in); any other exception fails the call with `Name: message` and is reported. A result the server refuses as too large fails the call. Answers are sent again after a 5xx or a network error; an answer to a task that is gone is dropped. A 400, 401, 403 or 404 on a claim stops the worker | Treating every exception as retryable | The script decides what to do with a `ToolError`; a retry is the tool's call to make. A refused key or toolset will not heal by polling. |
| `worker.stop()` lets the claim in flight finish and executes its tasks, then waits for the handlers | Aborting the long-poll | A task claimed by a request whose answer is lost waits for its claim to expire, and an `at_most_once` call then fails with `outcomeUnknown`. |
| After `ctx.defer()`, the handler's return value is not an answer (whoever holds the token answers), but a throw still fails the task | Ignoring the throw | The task would stay `deferred` until its timeout although the hand-over failed. |
| Pending approvals are read from `Run.waitingFor` (state `approval`, or `parked` by `schema_changed`); the arguments of a waiting call are read from the event stream (its `schedule_tool_call` entry) | Adding the arguments to `Waiting` | The API is frozen; `Waiting` has no arguments. |
| The agent tools answer the model in plain text. `get_run` also takes `offset` (pages of 256 KiB through the output, or through an entry with `entry`) and `wait`; `test_script`'s scenario is the fields of `POST /v1/test`, plus `from_run`, whose cassette is exported for the test | JSON results; no paging | Paging is how the model reads an output above 256 KB. A recorded run is the natural cassette for testing a corrected script. |
| The model's tool call id is the `Idempotency-Key` of `run_script`, `retry_run` and `signal_run`; an id the server would refuse is replaced by its SHA-256 | No key | Handling a tool call again must not start a second run nor send a second signal. |
| The agent tools check labels themselves: a run id without the agent's labels is answered as a missing run, before any signal, cancellation or fork; `retry_run` gives the fork the agent's labels | Relying on a key limited to labels only | The scope holds even with a broader key; a key limited to the labels still makes the server hold it. |
| A problem the model can fix (4xx: a bad argument, a refused script with its diagnostics, a run that has ended) is a tool result with `isError`; a 401, a 5xx or an unreachable server is thrown to the harness. The model's `wait` is bounded by `maxWait` (2 minutes by default; 30 s when it gives none) | Every error as a tool result | The model cannot fix the platform, and a harness must see its own misconfiguration. |

### Taken while implementing the tool tasks (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Backoff of a transient failure: `1 s × 4^(n−1)` after attempt n, at most 5 min, the upper half of it jitter drawn from `SHA-256({run_id}/{seq} # attempt)` (0.5–1 s, 2–4 s, 8–16 s, 32–64 s) | Random jitter; a fixed delay | The engine stays deterministic given its ports, and calls that failed together (an outage) come back apart. |
| Attempts count every claim, 5 at most. A transient failure at the 5th is delivered; a claim that expired a 5th time fails with `outcome_unknown`. Only a claim that expired is an interruption: a task claimed again after a retry, an approval or renewed credentials is sent | Separate counters per cause | One counter in the task, as the store keeps it; parking and approvals happen before anything is sent. |
| The engine renews the claims of the calls it dispatches (`heartbeat_tool_claims`, called by the worker loop about every second; a claim is renewed once a third of its heartbeat timeout has passed), until the tool's execution timeout | A sleep port; a claim as long as the execution timeout | The engine has no runtime of its own. A dead dispatcher still frees its call within the heartbeat timeout, and a call past its execution timeout loses its claim even if the dispatcher hangs. |
| In virtual time the driver jumps to the next retry, as to a timer: the earliest `next_attempt_at` of the `ready` tasks still to come, which the store returns (`next_tool_attempt(after)`, port addition, changed while integrating) | The engine keeping these times in memory | The store already holds them. An engine restarted during a backoff, or another engine on the same store, still wakes up for the retry. |
| `JournalStore::tool_task(token)`: the task holding a claim's token (port addition) | Token-only use cases | `/fail {retryable}` and `/defer` carry only the token; the policy, the attempt and the run's deadline are in the task. |
| `ToolTask` and `ToolCall` carry `schema_change_approved`, set by `approve_tool_task` on a task parked by `SchemaChanged` (port addition) | Approving without telling the dispatcher | The dispatcher would see the same mismatch and park the call again forever. |
| A parked task is reported (`tool.schema_changed`, `credentials.expired`, id `{run_id}/{seq}/{attempt}`) to the tenant's subscriptions after it is parked; a failed delivery is logged | Reporting before parking; through the feed | An approval sent in response must find the task parked. Parking is not in the journal, so the feed does not see it; the run's state still shows the parked call. |
| Credentials are resolved for MCP tools only. Given at start, they are stored before the run is created, and deleted when the start creates no run (an idempotency key already used, the quota) | Resolving for every route; storing after creation | Secrets go only where the transport needs them; a worker may dispatch the first call at once. Superseded by [secrets and API toolsets](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026). |
| The engine answers the `webhook` built-in of a live run itself: args `{"name": string}`, result `{"url": "{public_url}/hooks/{token}"}`, `unavailable` without a configured public URL. In mock mode the mocks answer it | A dispatcher adapter | Webhook tokens live in the `JournalStore`, which no adapter holds. |
| Host tools: a retryable failure is retried under `retry` with attempts left, otherwise delivered as `unavailable`; a non-retryable one as `error`. A deferral ends at the run's deadline, or earlier at the requested timeout or the tool's execution timeout from now. A token of another tenant is gone | Keeping the worker's failure kind | The API's failure carries no kind. The claim's start is not stored, so an execution timeout cannot be counted from it. |
| A result above `max_payload_bytes` fails a dispatched call with `error`, and is refused when a host worker completes (413) | Storing it | The script could not hold it within its limits; the worker can still fail the task. |
| `RoutingDispatcher { mcp, builtins, host }` sends each call to the dispatcher of its route; `Unrouted` fails the calls of a route without one (`unavailable`) | One dispatcher for every route | Composition roots plug `rewind-mcp` (`McpDispatcher` and `HttpTool`, changed while integrating the tools) and mocks per route; host tools only reach a dispatcher in mock mode. |

### Taken while building the dashboard (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| React 19, Vite, TypeScript 7 in strict mode, pnpm; types from `openapi-typescript`, calls through `openapi-fetch`, caching with TanStack Query, routes with React Router; Biome for lint and format | A hand-written client; ESLint | The generated types follow the API's diff. `typescript-eslint` needs TypeScript 5's compiler API, which TypeScript 7 does not have. |
| Routes in the URL fragment and relative paths in the build; `dashboard/dist/` is not committed, `rewind-server` embeds it when compiled | History routes with a fallback route on the server; committing the build | The server serves a plain directory under any prefix. Hashed build files would change in every diff. Superseded by [the plain URLs](#taken-for-the-dashboards-plain-urls-november-2026) for the routes: the build is served at the root, with a fallback to `index.html`. |
| The event stream is read with `fetch` and `eventsource-parser`, resumed with `from` after a drop | `EventSource` | `EventSource` cannot send the `Authorization` header. |
| The operator types an API key; it stays in `sessionStorage`, or `localStorage` when asked; the dashboard only sends `GET` | A login page and a cookie on the server | The API has no session: a cookie would be a second way in. A `reader` key is enough. Superseded by [accounts and access](#taken-for-accounts-organizations-and-access-november-2026). |
| The timeline compresses a gap into a marked break when it is longer than 30 s and four times longer than all the shorter gaps together | A linear or a logarithmic axis | A run that sleeps 10 days must still show its 300 ms calls, and a slow call must still look slow. |
| The source is highlighted with Lezer's TypeScript grammar | Shiki; Monaco | Small and synchronous, and it gives tokens line by line, which the notes beside each line need. |
| The mocked API is a set of MSW handlers over fixtures: vitest runs them with `msw/node`, and `pnpm dev:mock` serves them from the Vite dev server | MSW's service worker | Nothing of the mock reaches the build. |
| IBM Plex Sans and Mono are bundled from `@fontsource`, Latin only | Fonts from a CDN; system fonts | The dashboard loads nothing from another origin, and looks the same on every machine. Superseded by [the redesign](#taken-for-the-dashboards-redesign-november-2026): Geist Sans and Mono. |

### Taken while building the dashboard's sign-in and organization views (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The dashboard asks `GET /auth/config` first: an answer means the sign-in pages, a 404 the key form | A build flag; trying `/v1` without credentials | One build serves both, the embedded server and `app.rewind.codes`; 07 gives the 404 that meaning. |
| The client renews a refused access token itself: `/v1` answering 401 calls `POST /auth/session` once (concurrent refusals share it) and sends the request again; a second 401 goes back to the sign-in | Refreshing only on a timer; a refresh per refused request | A tab put to sleep misses its timer; one renewal for a burst of requests keeps the session's single refresh token in step. |
| The refresh is asked a minute before `expiresAt`, measured on the server's clock (the answer's `Date` header), at least a second apart, and 10 s apart while the server keeps the same token | The browser's clock alone | A browser clock ahead by minutes would ask continuously; the server refreshes only within its last minute. |
| An answer of `POST /auth/session` asked before a step of the member (a switch, a sign-out) is dropped when it arrives after it | Applying every answer | A refresh racing a switch would bring back the previous organization. |
| A stream closed by the server before the run ends resumes after the shortest wait, with the current token | Growing the wait at each close | The server closes a stream at its token's expiry, every 5 minutes on a paused run: that is not a failure. |
| The query cache is keyed by the member and the organization of the session (`user:<id>@<org>`), or by the key | Clearing the cache on a switch | It supersedes the cache per key with the same reason: nothing read in one organization is shown in another, not even while the other's answers are on their way. |
| A switch of organization opens the new one's home page | Staying on the same address | A run or a script of the previous organization does not exist in the new one. |
| The views gate on `GET /v1/whoami`'s permissions, less the administration ones when the grant is limited to labels; a 403 reads `whoami` again | Gating on the role's name; letting the server refuse | Roles are data; 06 refuses administration to such a grant whatever its role, and a grant changed meanwhile shows at once. |
| The role pickers offer the roles whose permissions the caller holds; the role editor disables the permissions the caller lacks; a member or a role beyond the caller cannot be edited from the view | Offering everything and showing the 403 | "No one gives more than they hold" is known before asking; the server still decides. Without `roles:read`, the role's name is typed. |
| Members, Invitations and Settings are shown in a member's session only; Roles and API keys also to a key | Showing them to a key with the permissions | `whoami` has no organization for a key, and those three are a WorkOS organization's. |
| A destructive action asks once more inline (revoke, remove, delete) | `window.confirm`; a modal | The question stays next to the row it is about, and works in tests. |
| A member's picture (`pictureUrl`, GitHub's avatar through WorkOS) is shown, asked for without a referrer, with initials when it does not load | Initials only | It is the person's own picture, given by the API; the rule "nothing from another origin" is the build's: scripts, styles and fonts. |
| The mocked world has one browser's session, held in the world rather than a cookie, and the mock's access tokens are opaque strings | Cookies through MSW | Node's `fetch` keeps no cookies under vitest; one session is what a dev server's browser has. The rules the mock enforces are the server's (06). |
| The first page of the sign-in opens the waitlist's form to someone new, beside GitHub and the code by email; a refused sign-in still leads there with its email | The form only after a refused sign-in | Someone new knows they are new; and how WorkOS refuses an unknown GitHub account is not measured, so a refusal may reach the page as `provider_error` or `sign_in_failed`, whose notice then points at the form. |
| The note of an invitation link says "invited to Rewind", not to an organization | Naming the organization | The waitlist's approval sends an invitation to the deployment, with no organization; the page cannot tell which kind it holds without asking WorkOS. |
| `pnpm dev:mock` signs in as with WorkOS by default; `REWIND_MOCK_KEY` asks for a key, `REWIND_MOCK_WORKOS=0` for nothing | The key form by default | The dashboard of 0.1 is reached through `app.rewind.codes`; the other two remain one variable away. |

### Taken while building the runtime (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The web-standard globals come from `deno_web` 0.291 (into which `deno_url` and `deno_console` were merged) and `deno_webidl` 0.260, both pinned exactly. The glue loads their scripts and installs only `URL`, `URLSearchParams`, `TextEncoder`, `TextDecoder`, `atob`, `btoa` and `structuredClone`; timers, `performance`, blobs, message ports and streams are never installed, and `URL` loses its blob URLs | Our own implementations; `deno_url`, which stopped following `deno_core` | Maintained and web-conformant. Their JavaScript and error messages are observable, so a new version of either crate is a change of the epoch. |
| The ECMAScript built-ins V8 ships stay, and the sandbox lib declares them: `Iterator`, `Float16Array`, `DisposableStack`, `AsyncDisposableStack`, `SuppressedError`. The checker's lib becomes ES2025 plus the ESNext pieces whose every declaration V8 ships (`array`, `collection`, `date`, `disposable`, `error`, `intl`, `temporal`, `typedarrays`), target `es2025`, still without `sharedmemory` and `weakref`, and without `decorators` (V8 has no `Symbol.metadata`). `WebAssembly` is removed. Checked with TypeScript 7.0.2 | Removing the five built-ins; the ES2024 lib | What type-checks is what exists. A test compares the isolate's global names with `globals.json`, with and without the `http` tool; another checks that every ES2025 and ESNext piece of `tsconfig.json` exists in the isolate. `WebAssembly` runs code the sandbox cannot see the source of. |
| Frames of the web globals' scripts (`ext:…`) are dropped from stacks like the glue's (`rewind:…`), in `fail_run` and in `console` | Keeping them | Their line numbers would leak into journals and change with the crates. |
| Each epoch has a V8 startup snapshot, built once per process at its first instance, on a thread of its own. It holds the isolate after the bootstrap; a per-instance `init` hook then sets the run's id, start, generator state and `fetch`, patches `Temporal.Now`, and removes `WeakRef`, `FinalizationRegistry`, `SharedArrayBuffer`, `Atomics`, `WebAssembly`, `Deno` and `__bootstrap` | A snapshot built by a build script; deleting them in the snapshot | A build script would compile the ops and link V8 a second time. V8 installs its flag-gated globals (`Temporal`, explicit resource management) only when an isolate starts from a snapshot, and fails to restore one whose shared-memory or weak-reference globals were deleted; `deno_core` reads `Deno.core` at restore. Measured in a release build: a new isolate takes 1.6 ms from the snapshot instead of 8.6 ms, for a 1.2 MiB snapshot built in 55 ms (`cargo test -p rewind-runtime --release --lib -- --ignored --nocapture startup`). |
| The CPU watchdog asks V8 for an interrupt; the interrupt reads the isolate thread's own clock (`CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`, through `rustix`) and terminates the execution once the budget is spent. The watchdog waits the budget minus the CPU time read last, and keeps one interrupt pending at most | Reading the thread's clock from the watchdog thread with `pthread_getcpuclockid` | macOS has no `pthread_getcpuclockid`; read on its own thread, the clock is portable and needs no unsafe code. CPU time never runs faster than wall-clock time on one thread, so a script never descheduled is interrupted about once. |
| Args are validated with the `jsonschema` crate, pinned exactly and without default features (no `$ref` is fetched from the network or the disk). A schema `{}` or `true` is not compiled. Every problem is listed, sorted, at most 5, at a path written as in the script (`args.labels[0]`, `args["a-b"]`). A schema that cannot be compiled refuses every call of its tool | `boon`; the first problem only; sending a call unchecked when its schema is unusable | Maintained, drafts 4 to 2020-12. Sorting keeps the message independent of the crate's hash maps; its messages reach scripts, so its version is part of the epoch. A call is never sent unchecked. |
| A call refused before it is recorded (invalid args, a tool or a built-in the toolset does not have, args that are not JSON) rejects the tool's promise with a `TypeError` (`RangeError` for too many commands) whose stack starts at the script's call | A synchronous throw from the op | Tool functions are async: `tool(args).catch(…)` must catch it. Nothing is recorded, so replay refuses it again identically. |
| `webhook(name)` calls the built-in `webhook` with `{"name": string}` and resolves to its result, `{"url": string}`. A toolset without the built-in makes it reject with a `TypeError`. `rewind_runtime::builtins` holds the input schema to pin for `webhook`; the schemas of `http` are `rewind-mcp`'s (changed while integrating the tools) | Exporting `webhook` only when the toolset has it | `rewind.d.ts` declares it for every toolset; the toolset decides whether the call is possible. |
| `fetch` sends the `http` tool `{url, method, headers, body?, bodyEncoding?}`: an absolute `http:` or `https:` URL, the method upper-cased when it is a standard one, headers as `[name, value]` pairs with lower-cased names in the script's order, `body` as text (strings, and `URLSearchParams` with its content type) or as base64 with `"bodyEncoding": "base64"` (bytes). It reads the result `{status, statusText, url, redirected, headers, body, bodyEncoding?}` of `rewind-mcp`'s `http` tool, of which only `status` is required. The body forms changed while integrating the tools (one format, below); a status outside 2xx is a `Response`, a refused request a `ToolError`. `Headers` are read-only, iterated sorted with repeated names joined by `, ` | Headers as an object; a body always in base64 | Pairs keep repeated and ordered headers; text keeps journals readable. |
| `rewind:tools` is generated from routes: one namespace per MCP server or host-tool group, host tools without a group by name; built-ins are not exported | Splitting names on the dot | A name says where a call goes; scripts reach the built-ins through `fetch` and `webhook`. |
| TypeScript mock handlers (`rewind_runtime::TsMocks`) default-export handlers by tool name or by namespace; a handler gets the args and `{tool, runId, seq, attempt, idempotencyKey}`, returns a value or a promise, and fails the call with what it throws (`message`, `data`, `kind`). They run in their own isolate and thread, from the latest epoch's snapshot, with randomness seeded with zeros and the time of a `Clock` the dispatcher is given; they import nothing and cannot wait (a pending promise fails the call); `console` goes to `tracing`; `handles(tool)` lets mock mode try another source first | Handlers in the script's isolate; a mock file format of our own | They cannot touch the script's state, and what they return is journaled. |

### Taken while building the engine's activation side (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The journal document holds `run` (the run id), `script` (the TypeScript source) and `toolset` (the pinned toolset document) before its entries. v1 changed in place, before any release: the golden fixtures and the corpus were recorded again, only their first line changed. `rewind_core::JournalDocument` is the model, `rewind_format::{encode,decode}_journal` its stored form | Entries only, with the script and toolset fetched by digest | An export, an archive and a corpus case replay on their own: the script reads `run.id`, and replay needs the exact source and toolset. A reader can check them against the digests of `run_started` (`JournalDocument::check`). |
| The lease is renewed between the steps of an activation (before each call into the script, replay included, and before the commit) once `lease_renewal` (10 s) has passed, by the engine's clock | A timer task renewing in the background | The engine has no async runtime of its own, and each call into the script is bounded by its CPU budget (5 s), so steps are short. In virtual time the clock does not move during an activation, and nothing is renewed. |
| A cached instance keeps the run's state with it; a hit is a state whose history length is the record's, and costs no history load. The instance is taken out of the cache during an activation and put back only after its commit; an error, a lost lease or a quarantine drops it and gives up stickiness | Keeping the instance in the cache while it runs | An instance that delivered triggers whose commit failed is ahead of the store: it must never be used again. |
| `ScriptInstance::memory_usage` (a provided method, 0 by default) reports the memory an instance holds; the cache sums it against its 4 GiB bound. An executor that does not measure it leaves the cache bounded by its 500 instances | An estimate per instance in the engine | The bound is on memory, which only the executor can measure. A provided method changes no adapter. |
| Effects are derived from the commands of the activation that are still pending at its end | From every command of the activation | A fork's first activation copies calls together with their outcomes: those must not become tasks. The rule is the same for every activation. |
| `RunMetadata::parent` is a `ForkParent { run, at }` | The parent's id alone | The API shows a fork's `at`, and the fork's first activation needs it: the copy of the parent's history is done there, in the same transaction as the fork's first commit, whatever worker takes it, and the API role needs no V8 to start a fork. |
| A fork runs under its parent's epoch. Its `run_started` is recorded at the parent's start; the parent's triggers are copied with their `recorded_at` and values; the responses recorded are the fork script's own (its callsites, its logs). A parent's history ends for the fork at its first `cancel_requested` | The latest epoch; the parent's commands copied as they are | The fork must compute what the parent computed to follow it, at the same logical times. Its trace points at its own script's lines. |
| A fork inherits, command by command, the calls whose commands matched the parent's before `at`. Once it goes live, the outcomes the parent recorded for the inherited calls still pending are delivered in the same activation; the parent's later signals and timer fires are not reused (timers are derived again and fire at their `fire_at`) | Inheriting whole trigger groups; reusing every later trigger | A `Promise.all` whose last branch changed must not repeat the effects of the others. Only tool calls have effects outside the run. |
| A call carries its parent's key `{parent_id}/{seq}` when the parent scheduled the same call (same `seq`, tool and args) and recorded no outcome for it before its cancellation; the rule follows a chain of forks. `Engine::call_key` computes it at dispatch from the histories | A key stored in the tool task | It needs no new column, and a call that matches an outcome-less call of the parent is safe to deduplicate against it. |
| Commands copied from a fork's parent do not count against the limit of commands per activation | Counting them | The copy can hold a whole history in one activation; the history cap bounds it. |
| `Engine::compatibility(run, script, at)` (`rewind replay --script`) runs the fork's copy without writing anything, as the run itself (it reads the run's id), and reports where the script stops following the run and which calls a fork would reuse, call again, or fail with `outcome_unknown` | A replay verdict only | The agent learns what a fork would cost before it starts one. |
| The projector is its own struct, borrowing the journal, the read model, the span exporter and the webhook sender; the composition root drives it next to the engine | More type parameters on the engine | The engine stays the write side; the projector and the workers scale apart. |
| A feed item is applied from the run's whole history: spans are derived from all of it, and exported when they end within the item's range; what is still open when the run ends (calls, timers, waits) ends with it. The read model gets the values of the table tier loaded, not those of the blob store | Spans from the range alone | A tool call span starts in one activation and ends in another. Shapes are observed on most results without an S3 round trip. |
| Read model and span export failures leave the feed item claimed, to be applied again; a webhook that fails after the sender's retries is logged and not retried by the feed | Retrying webhooks forever | One receiver down must not hold back the others' events forever; the sender already retries. |
| `run.suspended` is sent when an activation leaves the run waiting only for the outside world (signals, host tools), named `{run_id}/{index of its last entry}`. `run.quarantined` is sent by the worker that quarantined the run (`Projector::notify_quarantined`): quarantine is not in the journal, so no feed item carries it | A feed consumer reading quarantines | Lifecycle events stay projections of committed entries where they can. |
| Span ids: the first 8 bytes of SHA-256(`rewind/span/` ‖ kind ‖ 0 ‖ run id ‖ n), the kind being `run`, `seq`, `fence` or `idx`. Sampling compares the right 7 bytes of the trace id with `sampled_per_million`. Mock runs are not exported unless configured; only allow-listed labels become span attributes | Untagged hashes; parent-based sampling | A `seq`, a fence and an index never collide. The right 7 bytes are random in a UUID v7 and in W3C trace ids. |
| Payload tiers are `PayloadTiers { inline: 1 KiB, table: 64 KiB }` in the engine's configuration; a payload is loaded from the `payloads` table first, then the `BlobStore` | Inline threshold only | The table tier of 06. |
| Mock mode: a cassette, then mock handlers, then static responses. Mock handlers are any `ToolDispatcher` with the set of tools they answer (`Mocks { handlers, tools }`). A `sequential` cassette answers a call with the next recorded call if it is the same; a call answered once gets the same answer again. No source: the call fails (`error`) with "no recorded response for `tool({…})`" | A way for a dispatcher to decline a call | The port has no "not mine" answer, and the TypeScript mocks know which handlers they export. |
| A scenario runs from 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z in virtual time by default, with seeded ids and seeds; the cassette's signals are sent first, at the time they arrived, then the scenario's; each signal drives the run until its time or until it is blocked | The wall clock | A report is the same on every run. |
| Retention jobs are a `Housekeeper` borrowing the journal, the blob store, the read model and the secret store (a deleted run's credentials go with it); the archive is written by a function the composition root passes (`rewind_format::encode_journal`). An archive holds large payloads by digest: they stay referenced by the run until it is deleted | The engine depending on `rewind-format`; inlining every payload in the archive | The engine depends only on the core. Payloads are deduplicated and already kept while referenced. |
| The LLM trace is rendered from the history alone: a header (status or what the run waits for, digests, start), then one line per call, timer, signal wait, signal, log and end, with its index, logical time, line in `script.ts` and the outcome folded in; payloads truncated to 60 characters. `--entry n` shows an entry with its payload in full | One line per entry | The model reads a call and its outcome together, and expands an entry by its index. |
| Corpus scenarios can fork their run (`fork: { script, at, input, steps }`), defer an answer (`{"$defer": true}`), and expect how many times each tool answered (`dispatched`) | Fork cases outside the corpus | What a fork reuses is observable by the script and must hold across builds. |

### Taken while building the embedded store (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `turso` pinned to `=0.8.3`, its last release that is not a pre-release, without its default features | The `0.9.0` pre-releases; the default features | Turso is in beta: each upgrade is a deliberate change. Its default `mimalloc` feature installs a global allocator, a choice that belongs to the binary, not to a library. |
| One connection per store. Each port call holds it from start to end; writes are `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transactions; a transaction left open by a dropped call is rolled back by the next call | A pool of connections | Turso has one writer at a time anyway. Calls never interleave, as in the in-memory reference, and the measured throughput leaves the store far from the embedded server's bottleneck. |
| Turso's durability defaults: WAL and `synchronous = FULL`. Measured on a shared 8-core laptop: `commit_activation` p50 0.22 ms, p99 1.3 to 2.2 ms; 1,000 to 1,250 activations per second from one worker, 1,100 to 1,500 from four, each activation with its tool task claimed and completed (`cargo run --release -p rewind-store-turso --example turso_load`) | `synchronous = NORMAL` | The journal is the only source of truth: a commit is on disk when it returns, and the cost is affordable. |
| `runs` is keyed by `id`; composite values (quarantine, lease, trace context; a task's route, policy and state) are spread over columns. A run keeps its oldest inbox item (`inbox_head`, received at `runnable_at`) and a task when it can be claimed (`due_at`), in indexes that hold each pick's full order | 06's single columns; grouping the inbox at each pick | Run ids are unique across tenants. The picks of `acquire_runnable` and `claim_tool_tasks` read an index in order and stay flat from 300 to 2,000 live runs, where grouping the inbox cost 1 to 14 ms per pick. |
| Secrets: XChaCha20-Poly1305 (`chacha20poly1305`) with random 192-bit nonces, for the data keys and the credentials. A tenant's data key is created with its first secret, in its own table `data_keys`, encrypted by the `MasterKey` given to `TursoStore::open`; the associated data binds a data key to its tenant, and a secret to its tenant and owner | AES-GCM; the data key in `tenants` | Random nonces are safe at 192 bits. A tenant without settings (the embedded `local`) still has secrets. A row copied under another key does not decrypt. |
| The read model keeps one `tool_calls` row per call, each column set by the entry that knows it, with the shape of a held result (`result_shape`); a summary keeps maxima and recounts its calls after each batch; `observed_shapes` merges the shapes of the calls still projected | Storing projected entries; one merged shape per tool | Idempotent and order-independent without keeping entries. A purge or a deletion removes a run's share of a shape, which a merged shape cannot give back. |
| `meta` holds `schema_version`, which the server cannot set, and the server's settings by name; virtual time is saved in milliseconds under `virtual_time` | A table of settings | One key-value table, as 06 describes it. |

### Taken while integrating the branches (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The Turso schema stays at version 1, changed in place: `runs.parent_at` holds a fork's `at` next to `parent`, and the task view reads `tool_tasks.schema_change_approved` | A migration to version 2 | No database was written by a release. |
| `next_tool_attempt(after)` counts every `ready` task, host tools included, and only times later than `after` | Only the tasks this engine's filter claims; times already due | A host worker claims a host tool's retry once virtual time reaches it. A due task that cannot be claimed (no free slot, or a host worker's) must not make the driver spin at the same time. |
| The V8 executor measures an instance's memory on its isolate thread after the start and after each delivery, and returns it with the reply: V8's total heap size, plus its `malloc`ed and external memory | Used heap size only; asking the isolate thread from `memory_usage` | The bound is on what the worker holds: a heap V8 has grown is held even when partly unused, and array buffers live outside it. `memory_usage` is synchronous and the isolate lives on its own thread. |

### Taken while building the blob stores (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| On disk, a blob lives at `{root}/{tenant}/sha256/{ab}/{digest}` (the embedded server's root is `.rewind/blobs`). A put writes `{root}/.staging/`, syncs the file, renames it without replacing a blob, and syncs the directory; `open` removes the staging files older than an hour | One `sha256/` tree for every tenant; a temporary file next to the blob | The port isolates tenants, and the layout mirrors the S3 keys. A tenant id cannot start with a dot, so the staging directory never meets a tenant, and a crash leaves its leftovers in one place. The journal names a digest once `put` returns, so the blob must survive a crash. |
| On disk, a tenant's directory writes each capital letter of its id as `+` and the letter in lowercase: `Acme` lives in `+acme`, `acme` and `local` in their own name | The tenant id as it is | Tenant ids are case-sensitive and the file systems of macOS and Windows are not: `Acme` read and deleted `acme`'s blobs. A tenant id holds no `+`, so no two tenants share a directory. |
| `rewind-blob` writes its files itself rather than through `object_store`'s `LocalFileSystem` | `LocalFileSystem` | It syncs neither files nor directories, and stages next to the target. The file store is about a hundred lines. |
| S3 goes through `object_store` (feature `aws`). A put is conditional (`If-None-Match: *`), and a second put of a digest succeeds without rewriting it; `delete` asks (`HEAD`) before deleting; with an access key in the configuration nothing comes from the AWS environment, without one the environment and the roles fill in what is missing | Unconditional puts; a delete that reports nothing | One round trip for a new blob, and no object is ever rewritten. S3 does not say whether a deletion removed anything, and the port does (two concurrent deletions may both say yes). A session token left in the environment cannot pair with another key. |
| The workers' cache is `CachedBlobs`, in memory, in front of any `BlobStore`: bounded in bytes (256 MiB by default; a blob above an eighth of it is not cached), CLOCK-Pro eviction from `quick_cache`, filled by gets and puts. Puts always reach the store behind it; a delete drops the entry; misses are not cached | A cache on disk; caching misses | Workers hold nothing a run needs and run sandboxed. A digest always names the same bytes, so an entry is never refreshed, only dropped. A blob cleaned up by another worker and put again must reach the store. |
| The S3 conformance test runs MinIO in Docker from Chainguard's images (`minio`, and `minio-client` to create the buckets); it is skipped only when Docker is absent | MinIO's own images | MinIO no longer publishes them on Docker Hub or quay.io. |

### Taken while building the type-checker (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| tsgo is the npm package `@typescript/typescript-<platform>` 7.0.2 (macOS and Linux, arm64 and x64), pinned in `crates/rewind-check/build.rs` by its npm integrity (SHA-512). The build script downloads its tarball from `registry.npmjs.org`, or reads the file named by `REWIND_TSGO_TARBALL` for an offline build, and checks the integrity; the crate embeds the tarball (about 9 MB). The first use extracts it to `<user cache>/rewind/typescript-7.0.2-<platform>/` (or a directory the composition root gives), through a staging directory renamed at the end | Binaries committed to the repository; a tsgo found on the `PATH`; extracting at build time | The integrity is the one every npm lockfile records for the package, so anyone can check it. Four binaries of 25 MB do not belong in git. Another version of tsgo would change the diagnostics. The tarball is a third of the extracted size. Processes extracting at the same time leave one complete copy. |
| Each check writes the script, the sandbox lib and `rewind-tools.d.ts` into a temporary directory and runs `tsc --project . --pretty false --noResolve --singleThreaded --locale en` there, with an empty environment; at most one tsgo per CPU at a time, each stopped after 20 s. The triple-slash reference directives of the script and of the declarations are blanked first, positions kept | Resolving imports; parsing the `--pretty` output | With resolution, `import … from "/any/file"` or `/// <reference path>` made tsgo read files of the host, and its diagnostics showed what they held. `/// <reference lib="dom" />` added the DOM: what type-checks would no longer be what exists. One thread and a fixed locale make the messages depend only on the files. Measured in a release build on a shared 8-core machine under load: 170 ms to extract, 0.5 s for the first check (macOS verifies the new executable), then 45 to 65 ms per check, and 14 ms per check with 8 in parallel (`cargo test -p rewind-check --release --test check -- --ignored --nocapture latency`). |
| The `rewind-tools.d.ts` of a toolset that enables the `http` tool starts with `/// <reference path="fetch.d.ts" />` (`rewind_check::HTTP_TOOL_REFERENCE`), which `rewind-codegen` writes; the checker then adds `fetch.d.ts`, and `fetch` is no absent global. The declarations declare the module `rewind:tools`; empty, it exports nothing | A flag in the `TypeChecker` port | The port takes the script and the toolset's declarations only. The reference says, in TypeScript, what the declarations need. |
| Diagnostics: columns count Unicode characters (tsgo counts UTF-16 units); the frame shows the line, the lines before and after when not blank, and underlines the span on its line (the rule's node; for tsgo, the token where tsgo places the problem), cutting a line over 160 characters around it; chained messages stay on indented lines. An `absent-global` diagnostic replaces tsgo's about the same name at the same place. A diagnostic in another file is an internal error when the declarations alone have it with an empty script, otherwise the script's, shown at 1:1 only when the script has no diagnostic of its own | tsgo's spans from its `--pretty` output; reporting every file's diagnostics | tsgo's advice for `process` and `require` (`npm i --save-dev @types/node`) is wrong in the sandbox. A conflict a `declare global` causes is reported on both sides. |
| The checker's rules are errors. `floating-promise` flags, as a statement, a call through an import of `rewind:tools` or to `fetch` with the `http` tool, to `sleep`, `signal` or `webhook`, to a function of the script declared `async` or returning `Promise<…>`, to `Promise.all`, `allSettled`, `any` or `race`, and an async function called at once; through `.then`, `.catch`, `.finally`, `,`, `?:`, `&&`, `\|\|` and `??`. `ignored-async-callback` flags an async callback of `forEach` and of the array predicates (`filter`, `some`, `every`, `find`, `findIndex`, `findLast`, `findLastIndex`). `absent-global` takes its names and hints from `globals.json`, and flags references in types and through `globalThis` too | Warnings; `forEach` only | `void` says not waiting is intended, so the error costs one word. A predicate reads a promise as true: an async one is always a bug. An absent global fails tsgo anyway; the rule adds what to use instead. |

### Taken while building the tools (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The `http` built-in lives in `rewind-mcp`, as 07 places it: `McpDispatcher` serves the `mcp` route of the `RoutingDispatcher`, `HttpTool` the `builtins` route, where `webhook` fails (the engine answers it) | A crate `rewind-http` | One adapter for the routes Rewind dispatches itself; 07's crate table. |
| `rewind-codegen` writes `rewind-tools.d.ts`, the prompt rendering, and each tool's line and declaration (for `search_tools`); the `rewind:tools` module stays in the runtime's epoch glue. `rewind-tools.d.ts` always declares `module "rewind:tools"`, and starts with `/// <reference path="fetch.d.ts" />` when the toolset enables `http`, as the type-checker requires (04). Built-ins are not declared in `rewind:tools`: scripts reach them through `fetch` and `webhook` from `rewind`, and the glue does not export them there | Codegen writing the module too | The module is part of the determinism epoch; declarations only serve the checker and the prompt, at the latest epoch. |
| `rewind-tools.d.ts` declares `module "rewind:tools"`: one `export const <namespace>: { tool(args): Promise<…> }` per MCP server or host-tool group, its named types in a merged `export namespace <namespace>`, a bare host tool as `export function`. Tool names stay as defined, quoted when they are not identifiers | One flat set of types | Two servers can each have their `Issue`; the exports are the glue's. Checked against scripts with TypeScript 7.0.2. |
| JSON Schema mapping: an object without `additionalProperties` (or with `false`) lists only its properties; `true` or a schema adds an index signature; an object schema listing no property at all is a map. Each `$ref` target is a named type of the namespace, shared when it and everything it refers to are identical, else prefixed with the tool's name. Required properties first; Pydantic titles dropped; `format`, bounds, `pattern`, `default` and `deprecated` go to the JSDoc. Unresolvable references (remote documents) are `unknown` | Open objects by default | A misspelled argument is the mistake a check catches most often, and servers ignore unknown arguments anyway. |
| Arguments are optional (`args?`) when no property is required; such a call sends `{}`, as the glue does for a call without arguments. The dispatcher sends `{}` for `null` args | Always required | `github.whoami()` is how a model writes it. |
| An MCP tool pinned with an output schema returns its `structuredContent` (else its text parsed as JSON, else it fails with `error`). Pinned without one, it returns `{ "content": [blocks], "structuredContent"?: value }`, blocks keeping only the fields the declarations give them (no annotations, no `_meta`). The declarations type it `ToolContent<T>`: `text()` joins the text blocks with newlines, `json<U = T>()` returns `structuredContent`, else `text()` parsed as JSON; the runtime glue adds both helpers when it delivers the result | Returning the parsed text; making up `structuredContent` | Faithful to the server, one copy of each payload, helpers that are pure functions of the value. |
| Observed types: a host tool's observed shape is its result type; an MCP tool without output schema gets `ToolContent<T>` with `T` the observed shape of its `structuredContent`. Marked `@observed` in the JSDoc and `(observed)` in the prompt | Typing the content wrapper by its shape | The read model folds raw results. A server that puts JSON only in text gets no observed type until a projection folds the parsed text. |
| Prompt rendering: an import line, then one line per tool, `github.list_issues({ repo: string, state?: "open" \| "closed" }) → Result · first paragraph of the description` (200 characters), named types written out within 160 characters (then deeper objects `{…}`, then type names, then argument names only), a line for `fetch` with the allowed hosts and for `webhook`, and one line explaining `ToolContent` | Argument names only, as in 04's example | Argument types are what a model gets wrong first; the evals decide between renderings. |
| MCP connections are pooled by tenant, server (its transport) and the SHA-256 of its credentials; closed after 5 minutes unused or beyond 256, never while in use; dropped when their transport fails. Credentials go into HTTP headers (marked sensitive) or the process environment. A stdio server starts with an empty environment, a fixed allow-list of the dispatcher's variables (`PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`…) and its pinned `env`; its stderr is discarded unless configured | A session per call; inheriting the environment | A session costs an initialize round trip, a stdio server a process. Other credentials must not share a session, and the dispatcher's own secrets must not reach a server it starts. With [secrets](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026), the attached secret's SHA-256; a signed server's connections by tenant and server, each request signed. |
| The schema check uses the connection's tool list, listed again after 60 s or on `notifications/tools/list_changed`. A call is parked when its tool is gone, when a changed input schema refuses its args (the `jsonschema` crate, without remote references), or when the output schema it was pinned with changed or vanished; schemas compare as canonical JSON | Parking on any difference; listing before every call | A new optional argument would park every running call; the script's code depends on the result's shape. A long pause closes the connection, so the first call after it lists afresh. |
| MCP errors: HTTP 401, with or without a challenge, parks the call (`credentials_expired`); an unreachable server, a closed connection, HTTP 408, 429 or 5xx, JSON-RPC `-32603` and the execution timeout are transient; `isError`, other JSON-RPC errors, another HTTP 4xx and a missing stdio command fail. The execution timeout bounds the whole attempt, and abandoning a call sends `notifications/cancelled`.
