The organization's settings stay a page: they are its content | Forms opening inside the page, above or between its rows | Asked by the owner: the page keeps its place and its layout, and a form is read alone. |

### Taken for the dashboard's type scale (November 2026)

From the owner's review of a run cancelled at its deadline (`dev_notifications`, 18 November 2026); the scale was validated on a before and after of the table of runs and the run's page.

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| One type scale, as tokens (`tokens.css`): 16 px for a page's section, 14 px for a card's title (600) and the controls (500), 13 px for the body and the code (500 for the word that leads a line), 12 px for labels (500, ink-2), metadata (400, ink-3) and chips, 11 px for axis ticks and keys only. No 700, no half pixel; the figures (a tile's number, the sign-in's and a dialog's titles) keep 20 to 24 px | A size per component | The CSS had twelve sizes, three of them half pixels. "Step 2 of 2" was a `<b>`, which the browser sets `bolder` (700, the only one of the app) at the body's 14 px beside 13 px buttons; and text without a size inherited 14 px, so a banner's second line was larger than its 13 px title. |
| The body is 13 px; the controls (buttons, tabs, the sidebar, the header, Find) say their 14 px | A 14 px body | What has no size of its own is read as the body, never as a control. |
| A cancellation is told as one, never as a failure: titled by its cause ("Cancelled at its deadline", "on request", "by its script"), with what the run awaited when it came and where (`signal "notify"`, L25), then `CancelledError` and its message; its mark is gray, a failure's red, an abort's amber. The step that ends the run says "Cancelled in this step", and the awaits the cancellation rejected | "The run failed in this step", "threw CancelledError", "failed at" | The model is unchanged: `cancelled` is a `fail_run` whose error is `CancelledError` ([02](02-journal.md#run-state), [03](03-execution.md#cancellation)). The page said "failed" of a run the table called "Cancelled". |
| The cause comes from the journal's `cancel_requested` on the run's page; in the table's card, from the run's details read when it opens: a run that ended at or after its `expiresAt` was cancelled by its TTL | A cancellation reason in `RunSummary` | No change of the API: the details already answer, and only the card that opens asks. |
| An activity still open when the run ended shows as cancelled (its note, its span, the inspector) | "waiting", "in flight" | A terminal status clears everything pending ([02](02-journal.md#run-state)). |
| A cell of the table of runs shows its labels whole, those that fit in about 44 characters (an id shortened by its middle, `6afc64fdf…048836`), then "+N"; and how the run ended as the error's name over its message. Each opens a card (Base UI's popover, on hover, focus or tap): every label, each filtering; the end's title, cause, line, call that threw and time, and a link to the run | Chips that shrink to an ellipsis; a `title` tooltip | The chips were cut to `dax:i`, and the error's name ran into its message. A card can hold links, and a tap opens it where there is no hover. |
| A stack is shown only when it holds more than its first line | Always a "Stack" to unfold | A cancellation's stack has no frame of `script.ts`: it repeated the title. |

### Taken for the documentation (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The documentation is a Mintlify site in `docs/`, served at `docs.rewind.codes` (the owner, 15 November 2026), deployed by Mintlify's GitHub app from `main` with `/docs` as its subdirectory | A site generator of our own (Docusaurus, VitePress) on Vercel | The owner chose Mintlify. It hosts, previews pull requests and serves the pages as Markdown to models; the repository keeps only MDX and `docs.json`. |
| Three tabs: what Rewind is, why and how it works (with self-hosting); the SDK, with what the model writes and the CLI; the dashboard, view by view. Written in English for harness developers; the pages never link to `archi/` | Publishing the specification | `archi/` is the spec of the code; the documentation says what a user does. |
| The examples are one pnpm package in `examples/`, against the SDK published on npm (pinned): harness code run by Node's type stripping, and the scripts a model would write under `*/scripts/`, outside `tsc` (the server checks them when a run starts). Each was run against a local server when written; CI type-checks the harness code | Linking `sdk/typescript`; one package per example; snippets in the pages only | An example is what a user copies, with what npm serves. One `pnpm install` runs them all. |
| The dashboard's screenshots are taken by `docs/scripts/screenshots.ts` (Playwright) from `pnpm dev:mock`, signed in as Ada in Acme, in light and dark, as lossless WebP (sharp); a page shows the one of the reader's theme | Screenshots of the hosted dashboard; PNG | The mocked world is complete and holds no private data, and regenerating after a change of the dashboard is one command. Lossless WebP is a third of the PNG's size. |
| CI checks the site with `mint validate` and `mint broken-links`, `mint` pinned in `docs/package.json` | Relying on Mintlify's deploy | A broken page or link fails the pull request that caused it. |

### Taken while building the release gate (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A release gate, `acceptance/release.sh`, runs before any release, locally: the criteria of 0.1 that need neither WorkOS nor cargo, one spec per area of the product, the lifecycle, the sweeps and the load test, against the tree built in release mode; GO only when every stage ran and every check passed, NO-GO when a check failed or did not run, PARTIAL when the command line left a stage out ([its README](../acceptance/release/README.md)) | Growing the criteria of `acceptance/run.sh`; a Rust harness | The criteria prove that 0.1 is done; the gate proves that a release breaks nothing, and must also try what a hostile caller or script would. Playwright, the SDK and raw HTTP are what clients use, and the suite already has their helpers. |
| The gate never uses WorkOS: the operator's key makes a tenant per spec file, with an `owner` key. Organizations, members and invitations stay with criterion 8, against WorkOS Staging | Signing in through WorkOS Staging in the gate | The gate runs offline from WorkOS and its rate limits; keys hold the same grants, roles and permissions as members. |
| Every check carries one kind: `@success`, `@failure` (a legitimate caller's mistake ends as specified) or `@attack`. An attack asserts the exact refusal and the absence of any effect: the state probed before and after, the fixtures' logs of what reached them, and, after a limit, a run that must still complete | Asserting the refusal alone | A refusal answered after the effect (a signal delivered, a call sent, a blob written) is a breach the status code hides. |
| Setup A runs behind an edge that routes as `deploy/edge/Caddyfile` does (`POST /v1/test` to the workers, the rest to the api server), and allows MCP servers and webhooks on 127.0.0.1 so the fixtures are reachable; setup B, with a deployed server's defaults, proves the egress refusals | One setup | One server cannot both reach the fixtures on the loopback and refuse the loopback. |
| A canary secret made for each run is set as credentials; every answer is searched for it as it is recorded, and every log and Collector file at the end. Every exchange is recorded, and a 5xx a check did not declare (`allow5xx`) fails the gate, as does a panic in a log | Checking leaks and 5xx only where a check looks | A leak or a fault in a path no check meant to test still shows. |
| Each problem type of `api/README.md` must be provoked by a full run, except `internal`, `identity-unavailable` and `last-owner`: the gate has no WorkOS, so no member, and keys never count as owners; the permission matrix must cover every operation of `api/openapi.json`, and hold and refuse every permission | Lists kept by hand | A new endpoint, permission or problem type without a check fails the gate. |
| The GitHub workflow `release-gate.yml` is started by hand and leaves the load test out (PARTIAL); publishing the SDK does not wait for it | Running on tags; gating `release-sdk.yml` | The owner runs the gate locally before a release, the load test included. |
| A host tool's `inputSchema` and `outputSchema` that cannot be compiled refuse the toolset (422 `invalid-toolset`); an MCP server's schemas are still taken as listed, and a call of a tool whose schema cannot be compiled is refused | Refusing the calls only, for host tools too | The harness writes host tools' schemas: it learns at registration, as `api/README.md` says. A server's tools are not the tenant's to fix. |
| A call of `require(…)` refuses the script at start, unless the script binds the name `require` itself (a function, a class, a variable or a parameter) | Leaving it to a `ReferenceError` at run time | 03 refuses "a `require`" at start; `rewind check` already reports it, and a start with `check: false` must refuse it too. |
| Without WorkOS, a layer answers 404 on every route of `/auth` before its handler reads the body or the cookies | The 404 from each handler | A handler's body was parsed first: a malformed body answered 400, which told a caller the route exists. |
| A body whose `Content-Length` is above the server's limit (16 MiB) is refused with 413 `payload-too-large` by a layer in front of every route, before the body is polled | Refusing it when a handler reads the body | The body was polled first, so a client sending `Expect: 100-continue` received `100 Continue`, started its upload, and raced the refusal into a reset connection; refused from its length, it uploads nothing (RFC 9110 lets a server that will refuse answer without `100 Continue`). |
| The worker role's scheduler claims, with the filter `TaskFilter::Interrupted`, the host tasks whose claim expired and that cannot be handed out again (`at_most_once`, or `retry` past the maximum of attempts), and fails them with `outcome_unknown` at once | Failing them when a harness's worker claims next, as before | 04 fails an interrupted `at_most_once` call at once; a harness whose worker died may never poll again, and the run waited until its TTL. The sweep goes through the claim, so a harness's worker polling at the same moment cannot also hand the task out. |

### Taken for workflow test suites (November 2026)

Designed with the owner on 16 November 2026, with diagrams ([testing](05-testing-and-observability.md#testing-and-mocks)).

| Decision | Instead of | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A test is a scenario without its script** (the owner, 16 November 2026): its world (input, tool answers, signals, start, seed) and what the outside must see (how the run ends, the calls it makes). Always end to end | Tests that name their script; unit tests of a script's functions | The script is a parameter, so a test written for one version runs unchanged against the next. Only what leaves the run is checked, so a refactor breaks nothing it should not. |
| **"Parent" is the previous version in a workflow's lineage** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Tests flowing from the run that starts a child | A parent's tests mock its children's outcomes; each child workflow has its own suite. |
| **Tests are JSON documents, stored as blobs in S3** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | TypeScript test programs with `expect()` | They are stored, diffed, shown and checked against schemas before running; a TypeScript test could assert on more than the outside, and a stale one is found only by running it. TypeScript mock handlers remain for answers that compute. |
| **The suite is part of the version document** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | A mutable suite beside the version | A version stays immutable and content-addressed: "this version passed these tests" never changes. A change to the tests alone is a new version. |
| **Saving a version runs its whole suite, with no way around it; retiring (a deprecated test) or updating (a test whose state or result is out of date) needs a reason** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | A warning, or a `force` a model would learn to use | Every test must be green to publish a new version. The reasons stay in the lineage. |
| **An unanswered call fails the test; answered calls `expect.calls` does not list are allowed** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Strict call lists | The world bounds what the script may touch; `times: 0` forbids a side effect; a fix that adds a harmless read breaks no test. |
| **`retry_run` of a run of a workflow runs its version's suite first** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Leaving it to the model to test first | A fix that breaks another behavior never reaches production calls. |
| **Tests of an ad hoc script are not stored** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Tests stored per script digest | Ad hoc scripts are not run again; their tests become a suite when the script is saved as a workflow. |
| **Tests are recorded from past runs, one per path, and join a suite only through a save** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | One test per run; recording every run automatically | Real-world coverage without near-duplicates; a save that ran thousands of tests would be unusable. |
| **The dashboard's view is Testing, with a sidebar of its own (the workflows, the suite of the version shown, its details); the version and the test are switched in the breadcrumb; a suite is a table; each test has its own page, every JSON value opening whole, colored, in a dialog; a script's page shows the suites of the workflow versions it is the script of** (the owner, 17 November 2026) | A view named Workflows with every version one under the other and each test opening below its suite; menus beside the breadcrumb | One version and one test at a time read better than one long page; the breadcrumb already names the version and the test, so it switches them. |
| **JSON values fold, one object or array at a time or all at once: in Testing's dialogs and cards, in a run's payloads and in the dialog that opens a value large** (the owner, 17 November 2026) | JSON as indented text | A long value is read by folding what does not matter. |
| **JSON folds with `react-json-view-lite`, styled with the dashboard's colors; a value opens whole up to 100 000 characters of JSON, its first two levels above, and as text above a megabyte** | A tree written for the dashboard; another tree library (`@uiw/react-json-view` is still in alpha) | A small, maintained library without dependencies, accessible (a tree, the arrow keys). Opening a large value two levels deep shows an array's items without rendering every nested node at once. |
| **Testing shows no test result** (the owner, 17 November 2026) | Results computed again on demand (a read endpoint running a version's suite on the workers); the gate's reports stored with the version | Every test of a suite passed when its version was saved; the page shows what a test gives and expects, not a run of it. |
| **A recorded test keeps the run's start and seed, and is read like the run** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Seed 1 for every test | Without the seed, a script that puts a random value in a call's args no longer matches its cassette. With it, the run's random values can be computed again, so the test is as sensitive as the journal. |
| **A recorded test lives as long as a version references it, and is erased with its namespace** (the owner, 16 November 2026) | Expiring with its run; surviving an erasure | It is useful because it outlives the run; an erased end user's data must go. The next gate leaves an erased test out, listed as retired. |
| A call is answered by the test's rules, then its mock handlers, then its cassette; `rewind test` adds `rewind.tools.json` last | The scenario's order (cassette, handlers, static responses) | A rule is written for this test and must win; a cassette is what happened, the fallback. Static responses are a project's defaults. |
| `expect.calls` is a list of `{ tool, args?, times? }`: `times` absent means at least once; `order: listed` compares the first call matching each entry | A map from tool to count | Calls are checked by their args, and a side effect is forbidden with `times: 0`. |
| A run that can go no further in virtual time ends a test with the status `active` | A status `waiting` | The run's status is reported as it is; a test of "waits for approval" expects `active`. |
| `stale` and `incompatible` are found without starting an isolate: tools against the toolset, `returns` against output schemas, top-level `args` keys against an input schema's `properties`; input and signals against the workflow's schemas. An expected output is not checked against the output schema | Checking partial outputs against the output schema | A partial value fails `required`; the run itself says whether the output differs. |
| The version document gains `parent`, `tests[{name, test}]`, `retired[{name, test, reason}]` and `updated[{name, from, to, reason}]`, each left out when empty | Always present | The digests of the versions stored before stay as they were. |
| A test is given to a save inline, by digest, or as `{ from, expect }`; `update` takes `{ name, reason, test? , expect? }`, `retire` `{ names, reason }` | Only inline tests | A model corrects a recorded test without reading its cassette into its context. |
| A path is the run's tool names in `seq` order, `signal:<name>` for each signal it waited for, consecutive repeats collapsed, then its status; recording keeps the newest run of each path, up to 20 tests by default and 100 at most, among the 1,000 newest runs the query selects | All runs of the query | A few hundred runs usually follow a handful of paths; the bound keeps a recording to one page of the store. |
| Only completed and failed runs are recorded | Cancelled and aborted runs too | A cancellation or an abort comes from outside the script: the script's behavior is not what ended them. |
| A recorded test's name is `recorded <run id>`; its expectation is the status, the output or the error, and each tool's count | Expecting each call's args | The cassette is matched `by_args`: a call whose args changed has no answer and fails the test, which says the same with less to read. |
| A test document is at most 4 MiB; recording skips a run whose test would be larger, and names it | Truncating it | A truncated cassette would fail for reasons unrelated to the script. |
| `tests` and `workflow_tests` index the blobs; tests are reserved, marked, deleted and dropped like payloads, and `put_workflow` refuses a version referencing a test whose row is missing or marked (`Conflict`) | No cleanup; deleting blobs without a mark | A blob delete cannot be fenced. Recorded tests no save took would otherwise accumulate. |
| `POST /v1/test` takes `{ script?, toolset?, tests?, workflow? }` and answers `{ passed, tests: [TestResult] }`; `POST /v1/workflows/{name}/test` does not exist | A second endpoint to test a candidate | One endpoint: with `workflow`, the suite runs against the given script or toolset, which is how a candidate and an upgrade are checked. |
| The gate and the fork of a run of a workflow run tests: on a server without the worker role they answer 503 when there is a test to run; the edge sends `POST /v1/workflows` and `POST /v1/runs/{id}/fork` to the workers, like `POST /v1/test` | Running isolates on the api role | Only workers run model-written code. |
| `rewind test [script.ts] [--tests file] [--workflow name] [--toolset file]` replaces `rewind test scenario.json`; a tests file is a JSON array of tests whose `world.mocks` and `world.cassette` may name files | Keeping the scenario file | One format for a test, in a file, in the API and in a suite. |

### Taken while building workflow test suites (November 2026)

| Decision | Instead of | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The engine takes the stored format of tests from the composition root (`TestFormat`: `rewind_format::encode_test`, `decode_test`), as retention takes the archive's encoder | `rewind-engine` depending on `rewind-format` | The engine does not know the stored format (07, principle 1). |
| A test's TypeScript mock handlers come through a `MockLoader`; `rewind-runtime`'s `TsMockLoader` runs them in an isolate of their own, its clock started at the test's start | The server and the CLI building `TsMocks` each | One way to run a test, in the gate, `POST /v1/test`, a fork and `rewind test`. |
| A save whose name, description, script, toolset, schemas and labels are those of the latest version, without suite changes, returns that version (200). So does a save whose changes, composed on the latest version's own parent, make exactly that version: the same request sent again | A new version, its parent itself; applying the changes again on top of the version they made | Saving the same workflow again changes nothing, as before suites. The SDK retries a save whose answer was lost: applied twice, its new tests would collide with themselves and its retirements name tests already gone. |
| `GET /v1/workflows?script=<digest>` lists every version of any workflow whose script it is, newest first, on one page (`workflows_with_script` on the store), reading the script from the version's stored document | A column and an index on the script; the dashboard reading every workflow's versions | A tenant has few versions: the scan stays inside its rows, without a migration. The script's page needs one request. |
| A test document has no TTL; the corpus case `red/ttl_too_long` stays with the corpus harness | A `ttl` field | A test describes behavior; a TTL is an option of a start. |
| `rewind test` runs on the server when the tests need what it holds: `--workflow` (its suite) or `--toolset <id>` | Fetching the suite and running it in the process | A run in the process needs the version's pinned toolset, which the API does not hand out. |
| Mock handlers that cannot load fail their test (`failed`, "the mock handlers cannot load: …") | 400 `invalid-request` for the whole request | In a suite, one test's handlers must not hide the others' outcomes. |
| A test given inline with an `origin` is refused (422 `invalid-test`) | Taking it as recorded | A recorded test is read like its run: only recording sets where it comes from. |
| An update gives a whole `test` or a new `expect`, never both; the replacement takes the inherited test's name | Renaming through an update | A test keeps its name along the lineage. |
| On a server without the worker role, `POST /v1/workflows` answers 503 only when there are tests to run (the parent's suite, or tests and updates given); the edge sends every `POST /v1/workflows` to the workers | Refusing every save there | A workflow without a suite saves where it did. |
| Four tests run at once; a run of tests takes at most 5 minutes of wall-clock time | Unbounded | Each test starts V8 instances; a gate must answer. |
| Recording reads the candidate runs from the read model (`search_runs` with the caller's scope, `workflow:` and the statuses), then each run's history | Scanning the journal | The query language and the scopes are the read model's; a run that ended a moment ago may be missing until the projector reaches it. |
| Postgres: a new version locks its suite's test rows (`FOR SHARE`) until its references are written; the cleanup marks only rows it holds (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), then checks the references again | One statement | A test is never marked while a version that references it is being saved. |
| A test document and a payload byte for byte the same share a blob: each cleanup leaves out a digest the other holds when it marks it, but not one reserved after the mark | Fencing the two cleanups against each other | It needs a script to output a test document exactly; the cost of the fence is not worth it. |
| The evals' agent is a caller limited to its labels: the workflows it saves carry them, and `get_workflow`, `record_tests`, `test_script`'s `workflow` and a save's parent see only those; `save_workflow` derives the toolset its later scripts use, as the SDK does | A caller that sees every workflow of the tenant | It is what an agent holding a key or an end user's token limited to its labels sees through the SDK. |
| The evals' `record_tests` reads the runs `list_runs` finds (the agent's labels, newest first) of the workflow, completed or failed; its `query` reads only `status:completed` and `status:failed`, and names other queries as unreadable | Projecting a read model in each episode | The world projects no read model; the repair tasks record runs by id, and a query by status is what a model writes first. |
| The regression of a repair task fails after every call its fix makes (`fix_workflow_regression`: every label is bought, then the one without tracking fails the run) | A failure before a write, or in a call's args | A `{ from, expect }` test keeps the failed run's world: a call its cassette does not answer fails the test, so a fix that makes a call the failed run never made cannot pass its regression test. |
| A task of the evals can name production runs of workflows (`WorldSpec::production`): after the conversation, the latest version the agent sees runs with the given input, against the fake servers, and the checker reads it | Telling the model to run the workflow | Whether the saved version works is checked as the next real run would check it, whatever the model ran. |
| A workflow of a task's setup can be saved on a toolset made from the world's (`tracker_before_upgrade`): the fake servers serve only their current tools | A fake server serving two versions of its tools | The server changed; what changed is the toolset the old version pinned. |
| Episodes count the tests the model wrote (`test_script`'s and `save_workflow`'s tests and updates) and the fixes the gate refused (`save_workflow` and `retry_run` refused by `tests-failed`); `save_workflow` counts as a script submission for the first-try check rate | Counting them only in repair tasks | 05 measures whether models write tests unprompted and how often the gate refuses a fix they believed correct; a model may write tests in any task. |

### Taken while integrating Dax (November 2026)

Dax runs two environments in one organization, separated by keys limited to `env=dev` and
`env=prod`. Its integration found that such a key could not use a workflow someone outside its
scope saved first, and could not run a host-tool worker.

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A workflow version saved under a grant limited to labels has them in its document (`"labels":{…}`, omitted when empty), so they are part of its digest: the same workflow saved under other labels or tenant-wide is another version, carrying its own labels, and `POST /v1/workflows` answers 201 for it; saving it again under the same labels answers 200 with it. A tenant-wide save writes the document it always wrote, so its version is unchanged. `JournalStore::put_workflow` takes the labels from the version. A version stored before keeps the labels its row was stored with | Recording visibility per scope: one version, a row per scope that saved it | A scoped save answered the version saved first, which its caller could not see, list, pin nor start (404). A version keeps exactly one set of labels, which the API shows; the `workflows` table keeps its key, without a migration; and the toolset a scope pins differs from another scope's, as their runs do |
| A grant limited to labels claims, with `tasks:work`, the host-tool tasks of the runs carrying them (`TaskFilter::Host { labels }`): Postgres checks the run by its key for each due task (`EXISTS`, `labels @>`), Turso reads the run's labels with the task and filters them in the claim's loop. The token endpoints answer 410 `task-gone` for a task of a run the caller does not see, as for a token bound to another namespace. It still reads no tool statistics | 403 for such a grant, with a worker key of the tenant | Toolsets are content-addressed, so two environments that register the same host tools share one toolset, and the toolset filter cannot tell them apart: a tenant-wide worker answered both environments' calls, the dev server's against prod runs. The scope is the one the grant already has on runs, `list_runs`, signals and cancellations |
| A version's labels and its suite are both in its document. Without a parent named, a save derives its suite from the latest version of the same name saved under the same labels, its lineage, and answers that version when the save made it already. A parent named may be any version its caller sees | The latest version its caller sees, whatever its labels | A tenant-wide key sees every environment's versions: its save would derive from the latest of them, `env=prod`'s, and the same save sent again would make a new version each time instead of answering the one it made. |

### Taken while designing the MCP server (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A connection's access token is accepted on `/v1` too, naming its organization with `Rewind-Organization` | Handing the caller to the handlers in process | The MCP tools are a client of `/v1`, as the SDK's are, and what runs model-written code reaches the workers over HTTP with the same Bearer. |
| The consent's endpoints are `/v1/oauth/consent` | Under `/oauth` | The dashboard reaches `/v1` on its own origin (the Vercel rewrite) with the person's access token, as every view does. |
| The request travels sealed from `/oauth/authorize` to the consent, for 10 minutes | A row per pending request | Nothing to keep or clean for requests no one answers. |
| The connection is created when its code is exchanged, from the choices kept with the code | At the consent | A consent whose code is never exchanged leaves nothing behind. |
| A result holds at most 80 000 characters, and `wait` is at most 200 s | The SDK's bounds | Claude Code takes 25 000 tokens per result by default, and claude.ai gives a call 240 s. |
| `org` takes an organization's id or its name | Its id only | People say names in a conversation; a name two of the caller's organizations share is refused, with both ids. |
| The MCP tools call `/v1` on their own server over the loopback address, with `rewind_api::Client` and the caller's Bearer; through the public URL for what runs on the workers | A second implementation of the tools' port over the router in process | One client of `/v1`, its authorization and its limits for every caller; a local hop costs well under a millisecond against tool calls of seconds, and the workers are reached the way the edge routes every client. |
| `get_workflow` and `record_tests` take no `toolset` over MCP | The optional `toolset` of the first table | `/v1` finds a workflow by its name in the organization; the argument changed nothing. |
| `/mcp` reads its body up to `/v1`'s limit before `rmcp` does | `rmcp`'s own reading | `rmcp` reads a body whole, without a bound. |

### Taken while building the agent tools and the MCP server (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Two ports in `rewind-agent-tools`: `Api`, the operations of `/v1` the SDK's set calls (runs, checks, tests, scripts, payloads, tests, toolsets, workflows), and `McpApi: Api`, which adds `list_namespaces` and `list_toolsets` for the MCP set; `rewind_api::Client` implements both | One trait with the two listings | The evals' world serves the SDK's set only: it would answer two operations no tool of it calls with a refusal. |
| The handlers of both sets share one implementation; the MCP set's (`McpTools`) is built per call with the call's toolset, no labels of its own and the MCP bounds, the SDK's (`AgentTools`) per conversation with its options. `list_orgs` is rendered from the organizations the host gives, by `McpTools::list_orgs` | Separate handlers per set | The texts the model reads must not drift between Claude and a harness. |
| The evals' `World` implements `Api` as the server answers an API key of the tenant `local`, in the namespace `local`, limited to the episode's labels: a run without them is not found (404), the runs it starts get its labels and their scope, a `wait` is virtual time the world is driven through (300 s at most, as the server), a signal or a cancellation is taken at once. It refuses with a problem what it cannot answer as the server would: a run list filtered otherwise than by label and status, a record query other than `status:completed` or `status:failed`, a toolset not derived from a base, a run of a workflow by name. Unlike the server for a key limited to labels, it reads the script of a workflow none of the caller's runs ran yet | Keeping the evals' own handlers beside the crate's | One Rust implementation of the tools, which the evals measure and Claude uses. Two texts the model reads changed with it: another agent's run is now the server's 404 (`get_run failed: no run …`) rather than the SDK's label check, and `search_tools` ranks the workflows a toolset pins, as the server does. |
| What the evals count moved to the episode: the tests a call gives (`test_script`'s `tests`, `save_workflow`'s `tests` and `update`, counted from the arguments), a refusal of the gate (`save_workflow` or `retry_run` answering with the outcomes of the suite's tests), and the first-try check from the result's `script` verdict (`Passed`, `Refused`, or none when the call failed before the checks), which `AgentToolResult` carries for any harness | Evals-only fields in the result | The verdict is what a harness measures as its first-try rate; the rest is read from the call and the result. |
| Over MCP, no idempotency key: a call's JSON-RPC id is not used | The JSON-RPC id as the SDK uses the tool call's id | Without session, the same id comes back in other conversations of the same client: a key reused would answer another conversation's run. |
| A result of the MCP set holds at most 80 000 bytes, so at most 80 000 characters: half for a page of output, a quarter for a trace, the rest for the parts around them; pages and their offsets are in bytes, as the SDK's | Counting characters | The SDK's bounds and offsets count bytes; a bound in bytes is also one in characters. |
| `org` is matched by id first, then by name without regard to ASCII case; two names equal but for case are ambiguous, refused with their ids | An exact name | People type names as they say them. |
| A refusal the model can fix is a result with `isError`: an `org` that names none or several organizations, a permission the caller does not hold in the call's organization (the message names it), an invalid or missing `namespace`, a 4xx of `/v1` but 401. A fault of the platform (a 401 or a 5xx of `/v1`, an API that cannot be reached, a host that cannot say who calls) is a JSON-RPC internal error; an unknown tool is invalid params | Every failure as `isError` | As the SDK throws faults to the harness: the model cannot fix them, and the client shows the call as failed. |
| `tools/list` lists a tool when one of the caller's organizations allows its permission, and answers `cacheScope: private`; the permission is checked again in the call's organization | A list per organization | MCP lists the tools once per client; what a caller may use is theirs alone. |
| `toolset` is an optional argument of `get_workflow` and `record_tests` and changes nothing; it is required by `search_tools` only, and optional for `check_script`, `test_script` and `run_script` (the default toolset, `webhook` alone, without it) | Required everywhere 07's table lists it | `/v1` finds a workflow by its name; the SDK offers these two tools only with a toolset, but sends none. |
| The descriptions of the MCP set are the SDK's, each followed by where its arguments come from (`list_orgs`, `list_namespaces`, `list_toolsets`); the mentions of `save_workflow` in `test_script`'s and `record_tests`' are rewritten for `retry_run` and `test_script` | The SDK's descriptions as they are | Claude has no `save_workflow`: a description naming it sends the model after a tool that does not exist. |
| The boundary of `rewind-mcp-server`: a trait `Connector` (`organizations()`, `api(organization, namespace)`, with `type Api: McpApi`); over HTTP, `http_service(connect, allowed_hosts)` takes a function from the request's `http::request::Parts` to the connector, the host having authenticated the request and put what it found in the extensions; over stdio, one connector for the session. The `Host` values the service answers are the host's (none: any) | The handler reading the Bearer itself | Authentication, `WWW-Authenticate` and the 401 stay in `rewind-server`, with the code every client goes through; rmcp's default accepts only loopback hosts. |
| `rewind mcp` serves one organization, `local`, with every permission (the socket's caller is the operator), sends no `Rewind-Organization`, refuses `--url` and `REWIND_URL` as a usage error (64), lets `--namespace` narrow the reads that name no namespace, and stops serving at the first Ctrl-C | Asking for a namespace at start | Each call names its namespace, as Claude's do over HTTP. |
| `Client::with_organization` sends `Rewind-Organization` on every request, beside `with_namespace`; `Client::remote` takes any Bearer | A constructor per kind of token | The server tells the tokens apart by their prefix. |

### Taken while building the dashboard's Connect Claude (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The types of the connections, the consent and `GET /v1/server`'s `mcpUrl` and `oauth` were hand-written in `dashboard/src/api/connections.ts` while the API was built in parallel; they are now the generated ones, named there, and the calls go through `Api.raw` | Adding the paths to the generated file by hand; waiting for the API | `src/generated/api.ts` stays what the generator wrote. A field the API may leave out (`lastUsedAt`, `uri`) is read with `?? null`. |
| The consent is a page of its own, outside the frame, as the sign-in is | A view inside the sidebar's frame | It is reached from Claude, often in a window of its own, and asks one question: the person sees whom they answer, not the dashboard's views. |
| The route a sign-in starts from is kept in the tab's `sessionStorage` before leaving for GitHub, and put back at the next load when the address is `/#/`, once, within 15 minutes; any route, not the consent's only. A code by email signs in on the same page, the route untouched | Carrying the fragment through `/auth/github`'s state | The fragment never reaches the server, and `/auth/callback` lands on `/#/`; GitHub's round trip happens in the same tab. A deep link to a run is worth keeping the same way. Since [the plain URLs](#taken-for-the-dashboards-plain-urls-november-2026), the route is a path and the callback lands on `/`: the route is still kept the same way. |
| A member without organization who opens the consent meets the onboarding first, told that Claude waits; the consent shows once they are in one, the address kept | The consent with nothing to tick | Nothing can be allowed without an organization; the request lasts 10 minutes, the onboarding takes one. |
| The organization open in the dashboard is ticked in advance with the widest access the person may give (Run when they may); an access not offered is a disabled option | Read by default | Claude is connected to run scripts, and Read alone fails its first `run_script`; the access is in view, and the person lowers it. Connect Claude still advises the least access that works. |
| The client's logo (`logoUri`) is never loaded; a client known by its metadata document shows a check, one that registered itself says its name is its own claim | Showing the logo | An image from the client's server would tell it who looks at the consent and when, and a logo is the easiest thing to fake; the build loads nothing from another origin but a member's picture. |
| Connect Claude says "Connected" for a connection that is new, or whose `lastUsedAt` changed, since the page's first answer of `GET /v1/connections` | Comparing `createdAt` with the browser's clock | Only the server's times are compared: a browser's clock may be minutes off. A person reconnecting an existing connection sees it used. |
| `GET /v1/connections` is asked every 2 s only for a member, on a server with an MCP URL and OAuth; TanStack Query stops while the tab is hidden | Polling for every caller | A key has no connection, and a server without OAuth makes none. |
| A toolset's host tools are found from its summary: its tools whose group is none of its servers nor a built-in (`http`, `webhook`, `workflows`) | Reading every toolset's declarations | `GET /v1/toolsets` gives names and servers, not routes; one request serves the whole list. |
| The first message names the toolset by its full id, picked by its servers and short digest | A toolset's name | Toolsets have no name, and the tools take the id `list_toolsets` gives. |
| Account › Connections changes the organizations, the access and the labels of each, in a dialog; in the organization open in the dashboard, an access the person's role does not give is disabled, elsewhere the server refuses it (403) | Opening a consent to learn the accesses | `whoami` gives the person's permissions in the current organization only; `PATCH` checks every organization. |
| Connect Claude is open to every caller: without `mcpUrl` it says MCP is not enabled; without OAuth, Claude Code's command sends an API key as a header and claude.ai's steps say they need a sign-in | Hiding it from keys | An operator with a key may use Claude too, through what `/mcp` takes without a browser. |
| The good practices keep Claude's confirmation on `retry_run` too, beside `run_script`, `signal_run` and `cancel_run` | The three alone | `retry_run` starts a run, as `run_script` does. |
| Organization › Connections is a member's part (`members:read` in a WorkOS organization), between API keys and Settings | Showing it to a key holding `members:read` | Connections are people's, and open the organizations of their members. |
| The mock makes the connection at the consent, as if Claude exchanged its code at once; `pnpm dev:mock` keeps a request of claude.ai waiting at a fixed address (`request=claude-demo`), and "Claude" uses a new connection 3 s after it is made | Mocking `/oauth/token` | The dashboard never sees the exchange; a developer and the documentation's screenshots need a consent to open, and a connection to see arrive. |
| The documentation's "Use Rewind from Claude" is in Introduction › Get started, after the quickstart | Under the SDK | It needs no harness: it is a way to use Rewind, not to build on it. |
| Connect Claude is in the account menu (…), beside Connections, not among the sidebar's views (the owner, 19 November 2026) | A view of the sidebar | It is set up once per person, as the connections are managed. |
| Every change that cannot be undone is confirmed in a dialog that says what happens, with a red button named like the action: revoking a connection, a key or an invitation, removing a member or an organization from a connection, deleting a role, revoking a namespace's tokens (the owner, 19 November 2026) | A "Yes" inside the row | The question and its consequence read before the click; the row keeps its layout. |

### Taken while building connections and OAuth (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Connections, their codes, tokens and sealed requests count in the world's time, as `/auth` does, whatever the server's clock | The server's clock | Their tokens are an OAuth client's, which lives in the world's time; a server in virtual time would expire an hour in moments. |
| Exchanging a code when its person has a live connection of the same client keeps that connection (its id, creation and the tokens of its other devices), replaces its organizations with the code's and adds the new tokens | A new connection at each consent, or revoking the former one | One connection per person and client: claude.ai's Desktop and mobile share a client, and Claude Code signed in on a second machine must not sign the first out. The dashboard shows one row per client. |
| `connections.expires_at` holds when its latest refresh token lapses, renewed by each exchange and rotation; the workers delete a connection 30 days after it, or a day after its revocation | Reading the lapse from its tokens | The tokens are deleted a day after they expire: the date must outlive them. |
| A connection's tokens are deleted a day after they expire, used or not; codes a day after they expire | Deleting a refresh token once used, a code once exchanged | Their reuse must still be recognized to revoke the connection. |
| A `client_id` `http://…` is a metadata document only at a host the operator lets through with `--allow-internal-mcp`; anywhere else it is `https` | A switch for the tests | The tests and an internal client need a local document; the operator already names the internal hosts it trusts, for the same egress. |
| A metadata document is kept as its `Cache-Control` says: `max-age`, 24 hours at most; `no-store` or `no-cache`, fetched at each use; nothing said, 10 minutes, the life of an authorization request | 24 hours when nothing is said | It says nothing of being kept: one connection fetches it once. |
| A `client_id` URL has a path other than `/`, no fragment nor credentials, and is written as it parses (no dot segments, no case or escapes the parse changes); the document names itself exactly, authenticates with `none` and holds no secret; its name defaults to its host | Normalizing the URL | The client metadata document draft: the id is compared as written. |
| A private-use redirect URI "holding a dot" holds it in its scheme or its host (`com.example.app:/callback`, `cursor://anysphere.cursor-mcp/oauth/callback`) | In its scheme only (RFC 8252) | Cursor's scheme has no dot, its host has; the design's example is Cursor. |
| Registration: `token_endpoint_auth_method` defaults to `client_secret_basic` (RFC 7591), grant types within `authorization_code` and `refresh_token`, response type `code`, 1 to 20 redirect URIs, a name of 200 characters at most, a site and a logo kept only when `https`. The cap of 10 000 counts the registered clients before writing, without a lock | An atomic cap | A guard against abuse, not an invariant: a race passes a few over. |
| `/oauth/authorize` requires `state`; a challenge is 43 to 128 unreserved characters; `resource` compares without a trailing `/`; a missing `response_type` is `invalid_request`, another one `unsupported_response_type`. The sealed request holds the client, the redirect URI, the challenge, the state, the resource and its expiry | Optional state | 07 lists `state` among what it takes; PKCE does not replace a client's own check. |
| The consent offers the person's active organizations (WorkOS), each with their grant's role and the accesses it gives; allowing needs an active membership and a grant whose role holds the access's permissions in each, at least one organization, none twice. The labels proposed are `via: claude` whatever the client | Offering the grants alone | The membership is WorkOS's and the grant Rewind's: both must hold, as at each refresh. |
| At the token endpoint, a registered client authenticates as it registered, a metadata document's client is public and sends no secret; `invalid_client` is 401, with `WWW-Authenticate: Basic` after Basic. A code is checked in this order: unknown, used (its connection revoked), another client's, expired, its redirect URI, its verifier; a wrong client or verifier does not use it. `scope` answers `offline_access` | Using the code at any fault | A stolen code tried with a wrong verifier must not lock its client out. |
| At a refresh, when the identity provider cannot be reached, 503 `temporarily_unavailable`, and the refresh token stays good | Refreshing without the check | The check removes the organizations the person left; Claude retries a refresh. |
| `POST /oauth/revoke`: an unknown token answers 200; another client's token is 400 `unauthorized_client` | 200 for both | RFC 7009 refuses a token of another client. |
| A connection's request without `Rewind-Organization` reaches only `GET /v1/whoami` (400 elsewhere, `/v1/server` included); a connection never reaches `/v1/connections` nor the consent (403): they are a member's | Its first organization by default | Nothing is pinned at connection (08, Claude as a harness). |
| `Rewind-Organization` is read for every caller: an id that is no tenant's is 400, another tenant than a key's, a member's or a token's is 403, in `whoami` too | Ignoring it for other callers | 06: other callers may repeat their own tenant. |
| A connection's permissions in an organization: those of its grant's role, within its access (`reader` gives `read`'s three, `harness` `run`'s five), that its person's role there gives; its labels are both grants', and contradicting values give no access (403) | Only the person's grant | Each bound is the spec's: the access, the five, the person. With [secrets](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026), `read` gives four (`secrets:read`) and `run` seven (`secrets:use`). |
| A connection's last use is written when the one read is older than a minute | A write per request | 07: the connection is read at every request, written rarely. |
| Removing an organization from a connection (`DELETE /v1/organization/connections/{id}`) requires `members:manage` and the permissions of the connection's access there | Every permission of its role (`harness` has 17) | It uses only those; the rule of 06 bounds what is taken away by what is held. |
| Removing a member deletes, in that organization, the grants of every live connection of theirs, before their membership in WorkOS | At their connections' next refresh | 06: removing a member removes their connections' grants there, at once. |
| `/mcp`'s authentication is `Running::authenticate_mcp(bearer) -> McpCaller` (principal and organizations, each with its id, name, usable permissions, a token's namespace and labels): a connection reaches the organizations where it has access now; a key or a token its tenant, named by the identity provider; a member's access token, or anything else, is refused | Returning the `Caller` of `/v1` | `/mcp` acts in several organizations per caller; `list_orgs` needs their names and permissions. |
| The OpenAPI document holds `/oauth` and `/.well-known` (tag `oauth`, OAuth's formats: their errors are `OAuthError` in `application/json`, 404 a problem), a security scheme `client` (HTTP Basic), and `Rewind-Organization` on every `/v1` operation that acts in a tenant | Leaving OAuth out of it | The dashboard and the SDK generate their types from it; the endpoints are the server's. |
| Without a public URL and WorkOS, `/oauth` and `/.well-known` answer 404 (a problem); no CORS on them | CORS for browser clients | Claude reaches them from its servers. |
| Postgres keeps one live connection per person and client with a partial unique index, and an advisory lock on the person and client while a code is exchanged; the code's and the refresh token's rows are locked (`FOR UPDATE`) while used. `grants` gains an index on `principal` in both stores (Postgres migration 7, Turso schema 7) | Locking the tenant's grants | Two exchanges of one code, or of one refresh token, are decided one after the other; a connection's grants never touch an owner's. |
| Secrets are 256 bits in base 62 (`rewind_ct_`, `rewind_rt_`, `rewind_cs_`, codes without prefix), ids 22 characters of base 62 (`cn_`, `rc_`); secrets and PKCE compared in constant time, stored as SHA-256 | Random UUIDs | As keys and end users' tokens are. |
| `GET /v1/server` answers `mcpUrl` (`{public-url}/mcp`, or null) and `oauth` (a public URL and WorkOS) | A route of their own | The dashboard's Connect Claude page reads the server it talks to. |

### Taken for namespaces' display names (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A namespace has an optional display name, the customer's label for the end user (`Acme Corp`), set and removed by `PATCH /v1/namespaces/{name}`, a JSON merge patch (`namespaces:write`). The name stays the key everywhere: paths, `namespace:`, `Rewind-Namespace`, tokens, webhooks. `PUT` does not touch it | Renaming the namespace; the display name in `PUT` | The name is the customer's id, carried by tokens, webhooks, queries and links: changing it would break them. `PUT` replaces the exceptions whole; a patch changes one field without reading the others. |
| A display name is trimmed; empty means none; at most 128 characters (Unicode scalar values), none of them a control character; not unique. Anything else is 400 `invalid-request` | Unique; the rule of names | Two end users may carry one company's name. A label is free text, on one line. |
| `PATCH` never creates the namespace (404); one being erased answers 409 `namespace-deleting`, an empty patch included. It answers the `NamespaceDetail` of the current month, as `PUT` does | Creating it, as `PUT` does | A label for an end user who never ran anything is more often a typo than a plan. |
| `GET /v1/organization/namespace-names` lists every namespace that has a display name, with it, by name, not paged (`JournalStore::namespace_display_names`); a request bound to a namespace reads its own alone | Display names only in the pages of `GET /v1/namespaces`; a path under `/v1/namespaces` | The dashboard shows display names wherever a namespace appears (runs, alerts, tokens), and the pages of namespaces are ordered by use, not by the names a view needs. `/v1/namespaces/{x}` is a namespace's own path. |
| `prefix` of `GET /v1/namespaces` matches the start of a name, case-sensitive as before, or of a display name case aside; `total` counts the same set. Case is folded in the stores' code, character by character (`DisplayName::fold`, `char::to_lowercase`) | A parameter of its own; SQL's `lower()` | One search box finds `user_2fQ81` and `Acme`. Postgres's `lower()` follows the database's locale (ASCII only under `C`): every store must match the same namespaces. A fold character by character keeps the fold of a prefix a prefix of the fold. |
| Postgres keeps the folded display name beside it, in `namespaces.display_key` (migration 8); Turso, which filters the namespaces in memory, keeps `display_name` alone (schema 8) | Folding in SQL | The prefix is matched by the statement that pages, with the code's fold. |
| `NamespaceFilter` gains `name`: a request bound to a namespace lists that one by name, its `prefix` and `above` still applying | The bound name as the prefix, its first match kept | A display name starting with the bound name would match too, and could take the page's only place. |
| The agent tools' `list_namespaces` shows a display name quoted in parentheses after the name, and says to give the name as `namespace`; the CLI prints it quoted after the name (`list`, `show`) and sets it with `rewind namespaces rename <name> [<display name>]`, which removes it without one | A column of its own | A model maps "Acme" to its id from the same line; the CLI's table keeps its columns. |
| The dashboard shows a namespace by its display name wherever one appears, its id in a tooltip on hover (`components/NamespaceName.tsx`); the names come from one read of `GET /v1/organization/namespace-names`, kept a minute and invalidated with every namespace write | The id beside every display name; a read per namespace shown | The id is noise to a person and stays one hover away; a list of runs names many namespaces at once. |
| A namespace's page tells how its runs went over the month from the endpoints the run views read (`GET /v1/runs/stats` with `q=namespace:…` and `bucket=1d`, `GET /v1/runs/failures`), then lists its runs of the month and its scripts with the tables of Observability and Scripts | A statistics endpoint per namespace | The read model already answers by namespace; one table per kind keeps one behavior. |

### Taken for the dashboard's plain URLs (November 2026)

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The dashboard's routes are paths (`/overview`, `/runs/{id}`), no longer in the URL's fragment (`/#/overview`). The server answers `index.html` (`no-cache`) to a `GET` or `HEAD` that matches no file of the build, outside its own roots (`v1`, `auth`, `oauth`, `.well-known`, `mcp`, `hooks`, `assets`) and not a file name at the root (`/favicon.ico` stays a 404); Vercel rewrites every path outside `/assets/` and the API's to `index.html` | Routes in the fragment | The owner asked for plain URLs. The cost: the build is served at the root of its origin only, no longer under any prefix. |
| Below the root, a missing path is a route whatever its last segment holds | Refusing a last segment with an extension | A namespace's name may hold a dot: `/namespaces/team.alpha` must load on a reload. |
| `/auth/callback` redirects to `/` or `/?auth_error=<code>`, `/oauth/authorize` to `{dashboard}/connect/authorize?request=…`; the app rewrites an old link with a fragment (`/#/runs/…`) to its path at load | Keeping the fragment in the redirects | The redirects name routes the app serves; links already shared keep working. |

### Taken while freezing the contracts of secrets and API toolsets (November 2026)

The contracts of [secrets and API toolsets](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026)
frozen in code before the workstreams build them ([09](09-roadmap.md)).

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A pinned `auth` left out means none, for a server and an API alike. An API's document's security requirement is resolved at registration into the pinned `auth`; `{"type": "none"}` exists only in the request, to set aside that requirement | Pinning the document's requirement; writing none | Every toolset pinned before keeps its document and its digest. The dispatcher reads the pinned `auth` alone, never the document. |
| The stored format's keys stay snake_case: an API's `base_url` (02 wrote `baseUrl`), an operation's `idempotency_key`; a parameter's place is `in`, OpenAPI's word | `baseUrl`, as 02 wrote it | Every key of the stored format is snake_case; camelCase is the HTTP API's. |
| HTTP basic pins as a secret in `Authorization` with the format `Basic {secret}` and `"encoding": "base64"`; `encoding` is left out for `plain` | An `auth` of its own for basic; a `{secret_base64}` placeholder | One kind of secret in a header; the encoding says how the value is written, not where. |
| An API tool's route is `{"type": "api", "api": <name>}`, and its `operation` (method, path, parameters sorted by name, `body`, `idempotency_key`) a field of the tool; only an API tool has one. Each path parameter is a `{name}` of the path and the reverse | The operation inside the route | Tasks copy their tool's route, which the stores encode; the operation is pinned data only the API dispatcher reads. Sorted parameters keep the digest independent of the reader's order. |
| An API's name shares the namespaces of `rewind:tools` with the servers': an API named as a server refuses the toolset. An API's tools are capped per worker by its `concurrency`, as a server's (`server_concurrency` of the task) | Separate namespaces | Both export a namespace from `rewind:tools`. |
| `rewind-core`'s `SecretRef` is what an attachment names (`org/<name>`, `ns/<name>`, relative to the run's namespace); the engine's `SecretId { namespace, name }` is a stored secret, its `SecretNamespace` the organization (`*`) or a namespace. 07's `SecretRef { namespace, name }` and `Secret { tenant, namespace, name }` are `SecretId` and `ParkedScope::Secret { tenant, secret }`; a value keeps the name `Secret` | One type for both | An attachment and a stored secret differ: `ns/github` is Alice's in her runs and Bob's in his. |
| `SecretStore::put(tenant, NewSecret { id, value, labels, by }, now)` answers `Created` or `Replaced`; `list` and `meta` take the first day of the reads they answer (`since`); `read` takes the reader, `SecretReader { run, namespace }` | 07's argument lists; 30 days fixed in the store | A store never reads a clock; how many days to show is the API's choice (30). The API answers 201 or 200. |
| The last read keeps the run's namespace: `secrets` gains `last_read_namespace` | The run alone | 04 shows the last read's namespace, and an organization's secret is read by every namespace's runs; a run may be erased since. |
| Who wrote a secret is a string: a principal (`user:…`, `key:…`, `connection:…`) or `token:<namespace>` | `Principal` | An end user's token writes its namespace's secrets and is no principal. |
| A signing key's `kid` and a master key's id are both the first 8 bytes of a SHA-256 in hex: of the public key, of the master key | The JWK thumbprint (RFC 7638) | One rule for both ids, and the engine needs no base64. |
| A private key is its 32-byte Ed25519 seed (RFC 8032), `Debug` redacted, never serialized; the memory store makes keys with `aws-lc-rs` from 32 random bytes | `ed25519-dalek`; PKCS#8 documents | `aws-lc-rs` is already built for rustls; a seed is what every Ed25519 library loads. |
| `secret_usage(tenant, secret, limit)` answers at most `limit` active and waiting runs with their totals, and each workflow's latest version with the servers it attaches the secret for | Unbounded lists | An organization's secret may be attached by every active run of the tenant. |
| `ToolCall` carries the run's namespace and labels and the call's API; `CallAuth` is `None`, `Secret { value, placement }` or `Signed { key, claims }`, the claims the engine knows (`iss`, `sub`, `labels`, `run`, `tool`, `idempotency_key`, `seq`, `attempt`; `jti` derived) | The dispatcher reading the `SecretStore` | Only the engine reads a secret, counts the read and parks `secret_missing`; the dispatcher gets what goes into the transport and nothing else. |
| Mock mode passes `CallAuth::None` and checks no attachment at start | Checking them as a live start does | Mock mode reads no secret and signs nothing (05). |
| Parked tasks are stored as `credentials_refused` and `secret_missing`; a task stored `credentials_expired` reads as `credentials_refused`. Postgres's migration 9 widens the check of `tool_tasks.parked_kind` to the new names | A migration of the rows | The same refusal; parked tasks of running runs keep their meaning, and a 401 parks a call on Postgres as before. |
| A stored role or token key naming `credentials:write` loses it: unknown permissions are skipped when a grant is read, as before; an owner gives `secrets:*` again | Mapping it to the three `secrets:*` | `secrets:use` attaches the organization's secrets, which `credentials:write` never allowed. |
| The engine's `AttachRights` (`NamespaceOnly`, `Organization { labels }` with `secrets:use`, `Version`) and `AttachmentProblem` (`Missing`, `TakesNone`, `Unknown`, `Forbidden`) are the check's contract; `secret-required` (422) carries `attachments[{server, problem, secret?, attachable}]` | A message only | The SDK, the agent tools and the CLI show which attachment failed and what to attach instead. |
| `RoutingDispatcher` gains the `api` route, `Unrouted` until `rewind-openapi` | A dispatcher per toolset | The routes are fixed and known when the server is wired. |
| The registration's left-out operations (`skipped`) are in its answer only, not stored | Storing them with the toolset | They are not part of the pinned toolset. |
| `/organizations` is a root of the server, not a route of the dashboard | The JWKS under `/v1` | 07 puts the public keys outside `/v1`, at the issuer's path, without authentication. |
| The e2e corpus's `credentials` step puts the secret the run attached for the server again, or approves the calls the server refused | Rewriting the scenario | `blue/credentials_renewed` keeps its scenario and its journal. |

### Taken while building the stores of secrets (November 2026)

The `SecretStore` of [named secrets and signing keys](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026),
the runs' attachments, `resume_parked` by secret and `secret_usage` on Turso and Postgres, and the
master key ring of the server's configuration ([04](04-tools.md#secrets), [06](06-storage-and-scale.md#tables)).

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Postgres's migration 10 and Turso's schema 9 drop the credentials with the data keys that encrypted them. A tenant's next secret or signing key makes a data key under the current master key, so `data_keys.master_key` is never empty | Opening each existing data key at start with the current key, then marking it | A credential cannot be named in the new model, and production called only host tools: nothing stays encrypted under those data keys. No key to guess, no data key without its master key's id. |
| The master key's id is kept beside the data key, in `data_keys.master_key` | `tenants.master_key`, as 06 writes it | The data key already has its own table ([decided](#taken-while-building-the-embedded-store-november-2026)): a tenant without settings has secrets. |
| `signing_keys.public_key` holds the key's 32 bytes; the API writes the JWK. Each read checks the `kid` against them | `public_jwk`, as 06 writes it | The port answers the bytes, the stores need no base64, and one spelling of the key is stored. |
| The envelope of the credentials is kept: XChaCha20-Poly1305 with random 192-bit nonces. A value is bound to `rewind secret\0{tenant}\0{namespace}\0{name}` (`*` for the organization), a private key to `rewind signing key\0{tenant}\0{kid}`, a data key to its tenant as before | AES-GCM from `aws-lc-rs` | The stores' cipher already, safe with random nonces; a row copied under another tenant, secret or key does not decrypt. |
| A store takes `MasterKeys`, the current key and the previous ones (`MasterKeys::new(current).with_previous(…)`; a `MasterKey` alone converts into a ring without previous keys). It opens whatever data key it holds the key of, and answers `UnknownMasterKey` for the others; opening the store never refuses | The store refusing to open | The refusal, its message and its exit code are the server's, in one place; the store stays usable for its tests and tools. |
| At start, and before a rewrap, the server compares `master_keys_in_use` with its ring: an id it was not given refuses the start (`StartupError::Setup`, exit 70), naming the id and `--previous-master-keys`. A previous key still holding data keys is logged as a warning, one holding none as removable | Refusing only when a secret is read | The old key cannot be removed too early (04). |
| `--previous-master-keys` (`REWIND_PREVIOUS_MASTER_KEYS`) takes keys separated by commas; the current key and repeated keys are left out. The embedded server ignores it, as it ignores `--master-key`, and refuses `--rewrap-data-keys` | A repeated option only | One variable holds every previous key in a deployment's environment. |
| `--rewrap-data-keys` is `rewind_server::rewrap_data_keys`: it opens the store, checks the keys, rewraps, prints `{n} data key(s) encrypted again with the master key {id}` and exits 0; `rewind_server::start` refuses the option | Rewrapping at every start | The operator decides when, and sees how many changed. |
| A rewrap lists the tenants whose data key is under another key than the current one, then runs one transaction per tenant: it locks the row (`FOR UPDATE`), leaves it if another server rewrapped it meanwhile, opens it and seals it under the current key. Two rewraps at once change each data key once between them | One transaction for every tenant | A short transaction per tenant never holds up the servers reading secrets meanwhile. |
| A read counts in the transaction that takes the secret's row lock (`UPDATE … RETURNING ciphertext`), and the last read stays the latest when reads commit out of order. A deletion waits for it and leaves no read behind | Counting after the read | A secret put again after its deletion starts without reads (the conformance suite). The reads of one secret serialize on its row, one short transaction each. |
| A tenant's signing keys are written under a transaction-scoped advisory lock of the tenant, and a partial unique index keeps one current key per tenant; `signing_key` takes the lock only to make the first key | Retrying on a unique violation | Two first calls, or two rotations, at once make one key each, in order. |
| A rotation deletes the tenant's keys retired 24 hours or more before | Keeping every retired key | They are no longer published and never sign again: a private key that nothing checks against is only a liability. |
| `public_keys` answers the current key, then the retired ones still published, newest created first | | The current key is the newest. |
| On Postgres, `put` tells a creation from a replacement by `xmax = 0` in `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … RETURNING` | A select, then an insert or an update | One statement, right when two writers race. |
| `runs.secrets` holds a run's attachments as a JSON object (`{"github":"ns/github"}`, `{}` without any), `jsonb` on Postgres | A table of attachments | Run metadata like `labels` and `scope`, read with the run. |
| `resume_parked` matches the task's server or API (`tool_tasks.mcp_server`, which holds an API's name too) against `runs.secrets` in SQL on Postgres, and in Rust on Turso. Tasks stored `credentials_expired` before Postgres's migration 9 are resumed too | | They read as `credentials_refused` (decided with the contracts). |
| `secret_usage`: a run waits when a call to a server or an API the secret serves is in `approval`, `ready` or `parked`; a claimed call is in flight and has read the secret already. The versions are each workflow's latest version, listed when it attaches the secret, by name | The latest of the versions that attach it | Starting a workflow runs its latest version: an older one still attaching the secret reads nothing. |
| `secret_usage`'s check is in the journal's conformance module (`secret_usage_answers_the_runs_and_versions_attaching_it`), outside `run_all`, and both stores run it | Writing it twice, in each store's tests | One executable contract. `run_all` takes it once the memory journal answers `secret_usage` (the engine-server workstream). |
| A master key's text is never quoted: the base64 decoder's message, which quotes a character, is dropped, and the configuration's `Debug` shows the ring's ids | | Never log or print a key. |

### Taken while building API tools and signed calls (November 2026)

The outbound workstream of [secrets and API toolsets](#taken-for-secrets-and-api-toolsets-november-2026):
`rewind-outbound`, `rewind-openapi`, and secrets and signatures in `rewind-mcp`.

| Decision | Alternative rejected | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OpenAPI documents are read as JSON values (`serde_json::Value`) | `openapiv3`, `oas3`, `utoipa`'s types | None reads both 3.0 and 3.1 (`openapiv3` is 3.0, `oas3` 3.1, `utoipa` writes documents); a toolset needs paths, parameters, bodies, responses, security schemes and `$ref`, and pins its schemas as JSON Schema anyway. |
| A schema's `#/components/schemas/<name>` becomes `#/$defs/<name>` of the tool's own schema, the component converted into its `$defs`, once; any other `$ref` is inlined (a cycle refused); `$id` and `$schema` are dropped | Inlining every reference; keeping the document's components | A recursive schema stays finite, each tool's schema stands alone, and codegen names the `$defs` as types. `$id` would move the base of the rewritten references. |
| OpenAPI 3.0 schemas are converted: `nullable: true` adds `"null"` to `type` (and to `enum`), a boolean `exclusiveMinimum`/`exclusiveMaximum` becomes the bound's number. The values of `example`, `default`, `enum`, `const` and `x-…` are copied untouched | Pinning 3.0 schemas as written | The validator reads JSON Schema 2020-12: a `null` the API accepts would be refused, and a boolean bound is not one. |
| An operation's tool name is 1 to 128 ASCII letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`, as MCP names tools; without `operationId`, `{method}_{path}` with each run of other characters an `_` (`get_v1_measures_id`) | Identifiers only | Operation ids such as `get-user` are common, and codegen quotes a name that is no identifier, as for MCP tools. |
| `operations` names tools (the `operationId`, or the name made from method and path); an entry the document does not have refuses the registration | Ignoring unknown entries | A typo would silently drop a tool. |
| Not args: the header `Idempotency-Key` (the operation's flag), `Accept`, `Content-Type` and `Authorization` (which OpenAPI says to ignore), the headers Rewind sets, and the header of the API's `auth`. A parameter given by `content` takes its first media type's schema | Refusing such operations | Rewind fills them; the rest of the operation is usable. |
| Parameters are written in OpenAPI's default styles: path and header `simple`, query `form` exploded (an object's fields as their own pairs); a path value is percent-encoded but for RFC 3986's unreserved characters, `/` included | Pinning `style` and `explode` | The frozen `ApiOperation` holds a parameter's name and place only; the defaults are what most APIs use. A `/` in a value never adds a segment. |
| A request body is read only from `application/json` (with parameters such as `charset`); another type, `+json` ones included, leaves the operation out (`skipped`) | Accepting `+json` types | Rewind sends `application/json`; an API that requires `application/merge-patch+json` would refuse it. |
| The output schema is the first of `200`…`299`, then `2XX`, that has an `application/json` schema | The first `2xx` only | A `204` before a `200` has no body to type. |
| Without `auth`: the document's `security`, else its kept operations' when they agree (an empty requirement aside); in a list of alternatives, the first Rewind can follow; only empty alternatives mean none. Operations that require different schemes refuse the registration | Refusing any list with an alternative Rewind cannot follow | "A key or OAuth" APIs are common, and an API has one `auth`. |
| `base_url`: the first `servers[]`, its variables at their defaults, a relative URL resolved against the document's; `https`, or `http` on the embedded server (and in the CLI); no user, password, query nor fragment; a trailing `/` trimmed | Refusing relative servers | Documents served by the API itself often name `/v1`. |
| Read parameters are sorted by name by `rewind-openapi` itself, as `Toolset::from_parts` does; paths, media types and the schemes of a requirement are read in sorted order, never in the document's | Relying on `from_parts`; the document's order | The dispatcher may get tools straight from the reading (the CLI, the tests): the same query order everywhere. The order of a JSON object's keys depends on how `serde_json` is built (`preserve_order`, which some dependencies turn on): a reading must not. |
| An OpenAPI document by URL: `GET`, `Accept: application/json`, 30 s, at most 2 MiB, any redirect followed up to 5 times with each new host checked again; an error names the HTTP status, never the body; a document from an internal address that is not JSON, or not an API Rewind can read, is refused without a word of it | 307 and 308 within the origin only, as for calls | No secret goes with it, and documents are often served behind redirects; a tenant must not read an internal service through registration errors. |
| The API dispatcher follows redirects itself: 307 and 308 within the API's origin, up to 5, each request signed again for its URL with the call's `jti` | Not following them; reqwest following them with the first JWT | A JWT binds `htu`, which a redirect changes; the hop is the same request, which `jti` names. |
| A signed MCP session refuses every redirect, naming why (register the URL it answers at) | Following with the first JWT | rmcp's client sends the request again itself, with a JWT whose `htu` would not match; a clear refusal at registration beats a 401 at each call. |
| `htu` is the URL as sent (WHATWG serialization, its query included, its fragment left out); `htm` is uppercase; `body` is absent without a body; `iat` and `exp` come from the wall clock, not the engine's | DPoP's `htu` without the query; the engine's clock | The query is part of what an operation does; virtual time must never reach a receiving server. `verifyRewindCall` checks the same (the `ts` workstream). |
| The `jti` of an MCP request that belongs to no call (`initialize`, listings, notifications, the stream, the session's end) is 128 random bits in hex | `{run}/{seq}/{attempt}` of a call in flight | One per request, and the reduced set names no run. |
| An MCP request carries a call's claims when it is the `tools/call` whose `_meta` holds the call's idempotency key, registered on the session while the call is in flight; the schema check's listing on the same session carries the reduced set | Signing a whole session as its first call | Calls share sessions; each request says what it is. |
| MCP sessions are pooled by an HMAC-SHA-256, under a key drawn when the pool is made, of the transport and the identity: a secret's value and placement; a signature's issuer, `kid` and end user. A signed session serves one end user; the registration's listing has its own | An unsalted SHA-256 (as before); one signed session for every end user of a tenant | A dump of the pool gives nothing to try short secrets against; a server that keeps state per session never mixes two end users. |
| A `Retry-After` (seconds or an HTTP date) of a transient answer is waited out by the API dispatcher when it ends within the execution timeout and 60 s; a longer one answers at once, with `retryAfter` (seconds) in the failure's `data`, for the engine | Changing `Dispatched::Transient`, frozen | The engine's backoff then adds its own delay: never before `Retry-After`. Holding the claim a minute is cheaper than calling back too soon. Honoring a longer one is the engine's (engine-server). |
| A `2xx` answer: JSON for `application/json` and `+json` (a body that does not parse: `{ contentType, text }`), `null` for none, `{ contentType, text }` for another type, `{ contentType, base64 }` when it is not UTF-8; no `Content-Type` reads as `application/octet-stream`. Above 5 MB the call fails (`error`). No answer (a timeout, a cut connection) is transient: the tool's policy decides | Lossy UTF-8 | A binary body must not be corrupted silently. |
| A failure says `{api} answered HTTP {status} to {tool}`, then the problem's `title` and `detail`, or the start of the body; its `data` holds `status` and those, the body cut at 2 KB. An API reached at an internal address gives the status alone; a 401 parks with the challenge, unless internal | One message for all | The script reads `data` as a `ToolError`'s; the model reads the message. |
| API clients: one per tenant and origin, kept (256 at most, all made again beyond), whether it reached an internal address noted for good | A client per call | Connections are reused; the origin is what the egress checks judge. |
